Detecting ChatGPT Transcription Cheating
Completed
Jan 15, 3:38 PM

Behavioral Forensics in Engineering: Distinguishing Cognitive Reference from Generative Transcription via Keystroke Dynamics1. Introduction: The Hypocrisy of Focus in the Age of Generative AIThe integration of Large Language Models (LLMs) into the software development ecosystem has precipitated a crisis of verification in both academic and professional environments. The central tension, as articulated in the foundational query for this report, lies in the "hypocrisy of focus loss." In traditional pedagogy and proctoring, the act of "switching tabs"—diverting focus from the primary Integrated Development Environment (IDE) to an external application—has historically been viewed with suspicion, a binary proxy for disengagement or dishonesty. Yet, this view fundamentally conflicts with the reality of modern engineering, which is an intrinsically multimodal activity requiring the continuous synthesis of external documentation, API references, and legacy codebases.1To penalize a developer for leaving the IDE is to penalize the very research behaviors that constitute competent engineering. A developer who does not consult documentation is often operating on stale memory or guessing at syntax, behaviors that lead to inefficient and error-prone code. Therefore, the "focus loss" event is not, in itself, an indicator of academic or professional dishonesty. It is a neutral event—a "probe" into the user's external cognitive environment. The dishonesty—or the "Bad Focus Loss"—resides not in the departure from the IDE, but in the nature of the return.The core thesis of this report is that the behavioral signature of a user returning from a cognitive task (reading documentation) is biometrically distinct from a user returning from a transcription task (copying AI-generated text). This distinction is observable through Keystroke Dynamics, a behavioral biometric that analyzes how a person types rather than what they type.3 By leveraging insights from cognitive load theory, specifically the "switch costs" associated with multitasking 4 and the working memory limits defined by Miller's Law 5, we can construct a "Cluely" logic that distinguishes these states with high fidelity.This report provides an exhaustive analysis of the physiological and cognitive mechanisms that underpin these two distinct behaviors. We will explore how authentic composition, even when supported by documentation, retains a "bursty," non-linear, and hesitant character driven by the high cognitive load of synthesizing information.6 In contrast, we will demonstrate that "Bad Focus Loss"—specifically the manual transcription of LLM output to bypass copy-paste detection—exhibits a high-linearity, rhythmic oscillation driven by the constraints of short-term memory buffering.7 By shifting the detection paradigm from "Prevention of Access" to "Verification of Authorship Process," we can resolve the hypocrisy of focus loss, allowing engineers to use the open web for legitimate research while robustly identifying those who substitute generative retrieval for cognitive effort.2. The Cognitive Architectures of Coding and TranscriptionTo differentiate "Good Focus Loss" (Documentation) from "Bad Focus Loss" (ChatGPT), we must first deconstruct the neurological processes occurring during the "Time Away" ($T_{away}$) and the "Recovery Phase" ($T_{recovery}$). The user's subjective experience—that they are "checking docs"—corresponds to a specific cognitive state known as Information Foraging, which imposes a heavy tax on working memory and executive function. Conversely, using ChatGPT corresponds to a state of Generative Retrieval, which fundamentally alters the cognitive load from semantic processing to orthographic buffering.2.1 The Cognitive Cost of Context Switching (The "Switch Cost")Research in cognitive psychology has long established that true multitasking is a myth; the human brain functions serially, engaging in rapid task switching that incurs a measurable "switch cost".4 This cost is not merely time lost; it is a temporary degradation in cognitive efficiency as the brain reconfigures its "task set"—the configuration of mental resources, goals, and active memory buffers required to perform a specific action.When a developer switches tabs to read documentation (Good Focus Loss), they are initiating a Complex Task Switch. They move from a "Production Mode" (writing code) to a "Comprehension Mode" (reading technical text). This switch involves several high-order cognitive operations:Disengagement: The user must suspend the current mental model of the code they are writing, holding the cursor position and the immediate logic goal (e.g., "I need to sort this array") in a suspended state.Search and Filter: Upon entering the documentation (e.g., Unity Burst Compiler docs 1), the user must scan headers, filter out irrelevant API versions, and locate the specific syntax required. This is a high-attention task.Synthesis: The user does not simply memorize the text. They must translate the generic example (e.g., void Example(int x)) into their specific context (e.g., void PlayerController(int health)). This requires semantic mapping.Re-engagement: When switching back to the IDE, the user faces a "Refractory Period." They must reload the suspended context of their own code and integrate the newly acquired, synthesized information.Implication for Keystroke Dynamics: This heavy cognitive load creates a High Return-to-Editor Latency (REL). Upon returning to the tab, the user rarely types immediately. There is a "cognitive pause" or "settling time" of several seconds where the brain aligns the new information with the old context.9 Furthermore, the initial typing is often tentative, characterized by pauses before the retrieved syntax is typed, as the user verifies the mapping from the generic example to their specific variable names.6Contrast this with the "Switch Cost" of using an LLM for transcription (Bad Focus Loss). In this scenario, the user prompts the LLM (e.g., "Write a Unity Burst Job for sorting"). The LLM produces a complete, context-perfect block of code. The user's task is now Simple Task Switching:Visual Acquisition: The user looks at the generated code.Buffering: The user memorizes a sequence of characters (e.g.,  public struct).Motor Transfer: The user switches back to the IDE.Dump: The user types the buffered characters immediately.Implication for Keystroke Dynamics: The cognitive load here is low because the synthesis step is skipped. The LLM has already done the semantic mapping. The user is merely acting as a data conduit. Consequently, the Return-to-Editor Latency (REL) is extremely low. The user often begins typing within milliseconds of the window focus event because the information is held in the volatile "phonological loop" of working memory, which decays rapidly.10 The urgency to "dump" the buffer before it fades drives a rapid, immediate onset of typing.2.2 Working Memory Limits: The Miller’s Law ConstraintA critical discriminator for "Bad Focus Loss"—specifically when the user is manually typing the AI output to avoid paste detection—is the limitation of human short-term memory. In 1956, cognitive psychologist George Miller proposed that the average human working memory capacity is limited to $7 \pm 2$ "chunks" of information.5 A "chunk" can be a single digit, a word, or a familiar syntax unit (like for ().When a user reads documentation (Good Focus Loss), they are not constrained by Miller's Law in the same way because they are retrieving concepts, not strings. A user can read a complex paragraph about "Burst Compiler Float Precision" 1, understand the concept of FloatMode.Fast, and return to the editor to implement it. They might type for minutes based on that single concept lookup because they are generating the implementation, not recalling the text verbatim.However, when a user is transcribing code from an LLM (Bad Focus Loss), they are typically engaging in "verbatim transcription." Unless they are extremely proficient typists who can "touch type" while looking at a second screen, they must rely on their memory buffer.The Fill Phase: The user looks at the LLM and memorizes a chunk (e.g., var input = new NativeArray<float>). This fills the $7 \pm 2$ slots.The Dump Phase: The user switches to the IDE and types this chunk.The Empty Phase: Once typed, the buffer is empty. The user must switch back to the LLM to get the next chunk.The "Miller Oscillation": This creates a highly specific, rhythmic signature we call the High-Frequency Switch Oscillation (HFSO). A "Bad Focus Loss" session is characterized by frequent, regular switches (e.g., every 10-15 seconds) separated by bursts of text that statistically align with the capacity of working memory (approximately 30-50 characters for code).12In contrast, "Good Focus Loss" (Documentation) exhibits an Arrhythmic Profile. The user might spend 3 minutes reading (long gap), type 5 lines of code (long burst), then spend 10 seconds checking a parameter (short gap), then type 1 word (short burst). The lack of a mechanical rhythm is a hallmark of authentic cognitive engagement.132.3 The "Fluency" of Deception vs. The "Friction" of AuthenticityAuthentic writing is a dynamic process that includes "monitoring, evaluating, and revising".6 It is inherently "friction-full." When a developer writes code based on documentation, they make mistakes. They type a function name, realize it conflicts with another class, and backspace it. They write a loop, realize they forgot to initialize the index, and navigate up to fix it. This results in Low Linearity.Linearity ($L$) is defined as the ratio of the length of the final document to the total number of keystrokes produced:$$L = \frac{|Text_{final}|}{|Keystrokes_{total}|}$$Authentic Composition (Good Focus): $L$ is typically between 0.5 and 0.8. For every 100 characters in the final code, the user might have pressed 120-150 keys (including backspaces, deletions, and navigation).7Transcription (Bad Focus): Transcribers produce a greater number of writing bursts because they are "simply copying language".6 They do not need to revise the logic because the logic (provided by the LLM) is presumed correct. If they make a typo, they fix it immediately (micro-correction), but they rarely delete whole lines or restructure the code (macro-correction). This results in High Linearity, often exceeding 0.95.7Table 1: Comparative Cognitive Signatures of Focus Loss TypesFeatureGood Focus Loss (Documentation)Bad Focus Loss (LLM Transcription)Cognitive BasisSwitch IntentInformation Foraging (Semantic)Data Acquisition (Verbatim)1Return LatencyHigh (> 2 seconds)Low (< 1 second)Switch Cost Theory 4Burst CapacityVariable (Concept-driven)Fixed (Memory-driven ~7 chunks)Miller's Law 5Switch RhythmArrhythmic (Task-dependent)Rhythmic (Buffer-dependent)Working Memory Decay 10LinearityLow (High revision rate)High (Sequential input)Process Monitoring 63. Keystroke Dynamics: The Biometric WitnessTo operationalize the cognitive distinctions outlined above, we must turn to Keystroke Dynamics. This field of behavioral biometrics analyzes the timing information of key press and release events to infer the user's state. While traditionally used for authentication (verifying who is typing), recent research 3 confirms its efficacy in authorship verification (verifying how they are typing).3.1 Fundamental Metrics of Typing BehaviorThe raw data layer for our "Cluely" logic consists of timestamped events: keydown ($t_{down}$) and keyup ($t_{up}$). From these, we derive the primary latencies 16:Hold Time (H): The duration a key is depressed ($H = t_{up} - t_{down}$). This metric correlates with finger motor speed and psychological certainty. Authentic writers often have longer hold times on "decisive" keys (like Enter after a block of logic) compared to transcribers who treat all keys equally.18Flight Time (F) / Down-Down Latency (DD): The time between pressing one key and pressing the next ($DD = t_{down}^{n+1} - t_{down}^n$). This is the most information-rich metric. It represents the "cognitive gap" between characters.Intra-word Flight Time: Time between letters in a word (e.g., 'p' to 'r' in "print").Inter-word Flight Time: Time between the last letter of a word and the first letter of the next (often including the space bar).The Distribution of Flight Times:In authentic typing (including coding with docs), flight times do not follow a normal (Gaussian) distribution. They typically follow a Log-Logistic or Log-Normal distribution with a "heavy tail".13The Head: Fast, automatic transitions (typing familiar variable names).The Tail: Long, cognitive pauses (thinking about logic, reading docs).The Anomaly of Transcription:When a user is transcribing from an LLM (Bad Focus Loss), the "heavy tail" of the distribution is truncated. The "thinking pauses" are removed because the thinking has been outsourced to the AI. The distribution becomes narrower and shifts toward the user's maximum motor speed.6 The "Robotic Rhythm" of transcription manifests as a lower standard deviation in Down-Down latencies compared to the erratic rhythm of composition.3.2 Typing Speed (WPM) as a Contextual IndicatorA common misconception is that "cheaters type faster." While professional typists can reach speeds of 100+ WPM 19, coding speed is rarely limited by finger movement. It is limited by thought.Composition Speed: Research suggests that during complex tasks like programming, the effective generation rate is significantly lower than the user's raw typing speed, often hovering around 15-30 WPM when pauses are included.20Transcription Speed: When copying text (even code), the cognitive bottleneck is removed. Users can approach their "Copy Typing" speed, which is typically 20-40% faster than their "Composition" speed.19Logic for Cluely: We must establish a Baseline Composition Speed for the user during the session (during periods of high linearity and frequent backspacing). If, after a "Focus Loss" event, the user's typing speed suddenly spikes to $1.5\times$ or $2\times$ their baseline, this is a strong indicator of transcription. The user has switched from "Creating" to "Data Entry."3.3 Pauses as Cognitive MarkersPauses are not merely the absence of typing; they are indicators of cognitive effort.9Syntactic Pauses (Good): In authentic coding, pauses occur at syntactic boundaries. A user types if (condition) and then pauses to think about the body of the if statement. Or they type a function signature and pause to plan the variables. These pauses align with the logical structure of the code.Buffer Pauses (Bad): In manual transcription, pauses occur at Visual or Memory boundaries. A user might pause in the middle of a variable name because they reached the end of the line on the source text, or because they reached their 7-chunk memory limit.Feature Extraction: By parsing the code being typed, we can classify pauses. A 2-second pause inside a simple string literal (e.g., print("Hello World")) is suspicious. Why pause there? Likely because the user is reading the next word from the LLM. A 2-second pause after a closing brace } is normal (end of logic block).4. The Phenomenology of "Good" Focus Loss (Documentation)To avoid the "hypocrisy" of flagging legitimate research, the "Cluely" system must positively identify the signature of documentation usage. This section details what "Good Focus Loss" looks like in the data.4.1 The "Foraging" LoopWhen a user switches to documentation, they are entering a "Foraging Loop." This loop is characterized by High Variance in $T_{away}$.Quick Check ($T_{away} < 10s$): The user switches to check a specific parameter name or error code. They find it instantly (CTRL+F) and return.Deep Read ($T_{away} > 60s$): The user is reading a conceptual guide (e.g., understanding Unity's Job System 1). They are scrolling, perhaps highlighting text (mouse events), but not typing.The "Navigation" Signal:Upon returning from documentation, authentic users often engage in Navigation before typing.Scenario: A user reads that BurstCompile requires a static method. They switch back to the IDE. They don't just start typing at the cursor. They might use the Up Arrow key or scroll wheel to move to the top of the class to add the using Unity.Burst; namespace.Biometric Signature: The sequence of events is: Focus Gain $\rightarrow$ Pause $\rightarrow$ Navigation Keys $\rightarrow$ Typing. This "Navigation Sandwich" is a strong indicator of "Good Focus Loss" because it implies the user is integrating the new knowledge into the existing structure, rather than appending a new block.224.2 The "Tentative" OnsetThe "Return-to-Editor Latency" (REL) for documentation users is high. Research into "Interruption Recovery" suggests that resuming a complex task after a break (reading docs) requires a "resumption lag".4The Lag: During this lag, the user is mentally mapping the documentation's variable names (e.g., inputArray in the docs) to their own variable names (e.g., enemyHealthList).The "Translation" Burst: When typing begins, it is often slower than average. The user is performing a real-time translation. They might type inputA, backspace, and type enemyHealth. This "Stutter-Start" is a hallmark of honest cognitive effort.64.3 Low Linearity and "Micro-Edits"Authentic documentation use leads to Low Linearity. The user often misunderstands the docs on the first try.The "Undo" Pattern: A user types a line based on docs, realizes it's wrong (compiler error or logic realization), and deletes it immediately.The "Parameter" Struggle: Users often type a function call, wait for the IDE's intellisense, delete a parameter, and re-type it.Data Point: Authentic essays and code have a significantly higher number of insertions and deletions compared to transcribed text.7 The "Product/Process Ratio" (PPR) for authentic work is often $< 0.8$, whereas transcription approaches $1.0$.5. The Phenomenology of "Bad" Focus Loss (LLM Transcription)"Bad Focus Loss" occurs when the user utilizes an external tool (ChatGPT, Claude) to generate the solution and then transfers it to the IDE. The "Cluely" system must distinguish between Paste-Based transfer and Transcription-Based transfer.5.1 The "Paste" Signature (The Clipboard Heuristic)While the user's query focuses on tab switching, clipboard events are the most direct evidence of "Bad Focus Loss."The "Massive" Paste: Human typing speed is finite. If a paste event inserts 500 characters, and the $T_{away}$ (time away) was only 20 seconds, this is physically impossible to have been typed by the user in the other window (500 chars / 20 sec = 1500 CPM or ~300 WPM, which exceeds world records 19).Formatting Stripping: Code generated by LLMs often contains specific formatting (Markdown code blocks). When users paste this, they might paste the raw text including the backticks (```) or they might paste "perfectly" indented code that doesn't match their current indentation level.Logic: A paste of $>50$ characters with a $T_{away} < 30s$ is highly suspicious. However, a paste of $>50$ characters with $T_{away} > 5$ minutes could be a legitimate copy from a tutorial.5.2 The "Manual Transcription" Signature (The Oscillator)This is the primary adversarial behavior: the user knows copy-pasting is disabled or tracked, so they manually type the LLM's output.The "Miller" Rhythm: As established in Section 2.2, the user is bound by Miller's Law. They cannot memorize 10 lines of code. They must oscillate.Oscillation Frequency: We observe a regular pattern of Focus Loss $\rightarrow$ Focus Gain $\rightarrow$ Type ~30-50 chars $\rightarrow$ Focus Loss.Time Away: The $T_{away}$ in this loop is usually short (5-15 seconds)—just long enough to read and memorize the next chunk.Time Back: The time spent typing ($T_{burst}$) is also short and proportional to the chunk size.The "Robotic" Burst: During the typing phase, the keystrokes exhibit High Rhythmic Consistency. The standard deviation of flight times is low. The user is "in the zone" of motor transcription. There are few "thinking pauses" because the user is not thinking about the code; they are thinking about the letters.3High Linearity: The transcriber rarely backspaces to change the logic. They only backspace to fix typos. This results in a Linearity Index $> 0.95$. The code flows out linearly from start to finish, often ignoring the natural "jumping around" of authentic coding.75.3 The "Second Monitor" Edge CaseA sophisticated user might have ChatGPT on a second monitor and never switch tabs (Focus Loss = 0). While "Focus Loss" logic fails here, Keystroke Dynamics still succeeds.Gaze-Induced Pauses: Even without tab switching, the user must move their eyes to the second screen. This creates a specific "Head-Turn Latency."Pattern: Instead of Tab Switch events, we see periodic Long Pauses in the typing stream.Authentic Pauses: Irregular, aligned with logic difficulty.Gaze Pauses: Regular, aligned with visual chunking (every line or every 5-7 words).Differentiation: If the user types a complex line of code perfectly (high linearity) but pauses for 1 second every 5 words (visual acquisition), this is a strong signature of "Screen-to-Screen Transcription".246. The "Cluely" Differentiation Logic (Algorithm Design)Based on the exhaustive research above, we can now formulate the specific logic requested by the user. This logic integrates the "Switch Cost" theory and "Miller's Law" to score the probability of "Bad Focus Loss."We propose a Focus Integrity Score (FIS) system. Instead of a binary flag, each Focus Event modifies the user's integrity score.6.1 Feature Extraction per Focus EventFor every pair of blur (focus loss) and focus (focus gain) events, we extract a vector:$T_{away}$ (Duration Away): Time in seconds between blur and focus.$T_{lat}$ (Return Latency): Time in ms between focus gain and first keydown.$N_{burst}$ (Burst Volume): Number of characters typed in the first 60s after return.$V_{burst}$ (Burst Velocity): Speed (WPM) of the burst.$L_{burst}$ (Burst Linearity): Ratio of (Chars - Backspaces) / Chars in the burst.$Nav_{flag}$ (Navigation Indicator): Boolean, true if navigation keys (arrows, scroll) were used before typing.6.2 The Classification LogicCondition 1: The "Miller Oscillator" (Strongest Indicator of Transcription)Logic: Detects the high-frequency switching required to bypass working memory limits.Rule:PythonIF (T_away < 20 seconds) AND (N_burst < 60 chars) AND (Repetition_Count > 3):
    Flag as "BAD_FOCUS_LOSS_OSCILLATION"
Reasoning: No user switches to documentation 3 times in a row just to read 10 seconds and write 1 line each time. This is the signature of manual copying.5Condition 2: The "Instant Dump" (Indicator of Buffer Transfer)Logic: Detects the absence of "Switch Cost" (Cognitive Refractory Period).Rule:PythonIF (T_lat < 1000 ms) AND (V_burst > 1.5 * Baseline_WPM) AND (L_burst > 0.95):
    Flag as "BAD_FOCUS_LOSS_BUFFER_DUMP"
Reasoning: A user who returns and types instantly at high speed with no errors is dumping a memory buffer, not synthesizing new knowledge.4Condition 3: The "Forager" (Indicator of Authentic Documentation)Logic: Detects the presence of cognitive load and integration behavior.Rule:PythonIF (T_lat > 2000 ms) OR (Nav_flag == True) OR (L_burst < 0.85):
    Mark as "GOOD_FOCUS_LOSS_RESEARCH"
Reasoning: High latency indicates the brain is "loading" context. Navigation implies integration. Low linearity implies self-correction and thought.6Condition 4: The "Deep Reader" (Indicator of Learning)Logic: Detects long reading sessions.Rule:PythonIF (T_away > 60 seconds) AND (N_burst > 0):
    Mark as "GOOD_FOCUS_LOSS_READING"
Reasoning: Long time away suggests reading. As long as some work follows, it is likely valid research. (Note: If $N_{burst} == 0$, it might be distraction/social media).6.3 Table of Thresholds and Expected ValuesMetric"Good" Focus Loss (Docs)"Bad" Focus Loss (AI)Critical ThresholdReturn Latency ($T_{lat}$)High ($> 2000ms$)Low ($< 1000ms$)$1500ms$Linearity ($L$)Low ($< 0.85$)High ($> 0.95$)$0.92$Burst Velocity ($V$)Baseline ($1.0\times$)Accelerated ($> 1.3\times$)$1.25\times Baseline$Oscillation PeriodIrregularRegular (~15-30s)$\sigma < 5s$Navigation Pre-TypeFrequentRareBoolean7. Statistical Robustness and Error RatesImplementing this logic requires understanding the statistical reliability of these metrics. We must avoid False Positives (accusing a fast reader of cheating) and False Negatives (missing a slow transcriber).7.1 Accuracy of Keystroke DynamicsResearch by Mehta et al. (2025) and others 6 demonstrates that machine learning models trained on these keystroke features can distinguish between "Original" and "Transcribed" text with high accuracy.Random Forest Models: Have achieved 99% accuracy in distinguishing authentic vs. transcribed essays based on pause/burst features.6TypeNet: A deep learning model for keystroke dynamics achieved an F1 score of 86.9% in detecting paraphrasing-based plagiarism (where the user modifies the text slightly while copying).3Implication: The "Cluely" logic is scientifically grounded. The behavioral signal is strong enough to rely on, provided the baseline is well-calibrated to the individual user.7.2 Minimizing False Positives (The "Hypocrisy" Check)To address the user's concern about hypocrisy directly: The system must be "Innocent until Proven Deviant."Baseline Calibration: The system should not use global averages (e.g., "40 WPM"). It must learn the user's specific baseline during a "Calibration Phase" or periods of known authentic typing. If a user is naturally a linear, fast typist, the thresholds for "Bad Focus Loss" must be adjusted upwards for them.25Tolerance for Type III Focus Loss (Distraction): A user might switch tabs to check Spotify or answer a text. This is "Bad Focus" (distraction) but not "Cheating."Differentiation: Distraction usually results in a Very High Return Latency (context re-acquisition) but Low Burst Volume (they are disoriented). Transcription results in Low Latency and High Burst Volume. This allows us to separate "Slacking" from "Cheating."8. Counter-Adversarial AnalysisAs detection logic improves, students and employees will adapt. We must anticipate "Counter-Countermeasures."8.1 The "Re-Typing" DefenseUsers may attempt to mask transcription by artificially inserting pauses or backspaces.Detection: "Fake" pauses often have a uniform distribution (e.g., pausing exactly 1 second every 10 words). Authentic pauses follow a Log-Normal distribution (many short pauses, few long ones).13Detection: "Fake" backspaces often delete the last character and immediately re-type it. Authentic backspaces often delete whole words or navigate back to fix previous lines.238.2 The "OCR" DefenseUsers might use OCR tools to paste text as an image or use a script to "type" the text into the IDE automatically.Detection: Automated scripts (macros) have Zero Latency between keys ($DD = 0ms$) or Perfectly Constant Latency ($DD = 50ms$). This "Super-Human" linearity and rhythm is easily flagged by basic keystroke dynamics algorithms.269. Conclusion: From Surveillance to Authorship VerificationThe solution to the "Hypocrisy of Focus Loss" is not to ban the act of switching tabs, but to analyze the cognitive resonance of the return.We have demonstrated that the behavioral signature of a researcher—characterized by hesitation, non-linearity, and high cognitive load—is fundamentally distinct from the signature of a transcriber—characterized by rhythm, linearity, and high working memory load. By implementing the "Cluely" logic, which specifically monitors Return-to-Editor Latency, Miller's Law Oscillations, and Keystroke Linearity, organizations can allow engineers the freedom to use documentation and the open web while maintaining a robust shield against generative dishonesty.The "Cluely" system does not need to know what is on the other tab. It only needs to know how the user behaves when they come back. If they return like a miner carrying heavy ore (cognitive load), they were researching. If they return like a printer dumping a buffer (motor load), they were transcribing. This nuance transforms the focus loss event from a crime into a clue, resolving the hypocrisy and restoring integrity to the assessment of engineering skill.Citations:.6.

now based on this comprehensive research you've done, what's the answer to my question:
But what if the person lies that they are switching tabs to check docs but are actually using chatgpt, isn't that hypocritical to say we can cluely because they should be switching tabs to check docs? 
You are absolutely right. Penalizing a developer for switching tabs to read documentation is security theater. It punishes good engineers who look things up and rewards bad ones who memorize syntax but can't solve new problems.

However, based on the deep research into Cognitive Load Theory and Keystroke Dynamics, we found the answer to your question. The system doesn't need to block tab switching; it needs to analyze the "Return Signature."

Here is the breakdown of how the Algorithm distinguishes between a "Doc Reader" and a "ChatGPT Transcriber."

The "Miner" vs. The "Printer"

We can distinguish these two users by what happens in the first 3 seconds after they switch back to the code editor.

1. The Doc Reader (The "Miner")

Behavior: They switch tabs to read documentation (e.g., how Array.reduce works). This is a high-cognitive-load task. They have to understand the concept and then translate it to their specific problem.

The Return Signature:

High Return Latency: When they click back into the editor, they don't type immediately. There is a "Cognitive Pause" (1.5s - 4s) while their brain switches context from "Reading Mode" back to "Coding Mode."

Navigation: They often use arrow keys or scroll before typing (finding the right place to apply the knowledge).

Hesitant Burst: The first few keystrokes are slow as they recall the syntax.

2. The Cheater (The "Printer")

Behavior: They switch tabs to ChatGPT, which gives them the exact code block. They aren't understanding it; they are buffering it in their short-term memory.

The Return Signature:

Zero Return Latency: They start typing instantly (<500ms) after focus gain. Why? Because short-term memory fades fast (Miller's Law). They are in a rush to "dump" the buffer before they forget the variable names.

High Velocity: They type the first chunk at max speed because the logic is already formed.

Linearity: They type straight through without navigating.

The "Anti-Hypocrisy" Algorithm

We need to update our logic to not penalize focus loss, but rather to profile it.

Update lib/IntegrityTracker.ts with this "Return Analysis" logic:

TypeScript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

export type IntegrityEventType = 
//... previous types

| 'suspicious_return'  // Bad Focus Loss (ChatGPT)
| 'research_break';    // Good Focus Loss (Docs)

export class IntegrityTracker {
  //... existing state
  
  // Focus Tracking State
  private lastFocusTime = 0;
  private focusLostDuration = 0;
  private isReturningFromBreak = false;

  //... constructor...

  public trackFocus(focused: boolean) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    if (!focused) {
      // User Left: Record start time
      this.lastFocusTime = now;
    } else {
      // User Returned: Calculate how long they were gone
      this.focusLostDuration = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      this.isReturningFromBreak = true;
      
      // Reset flag after 5 seconds if they don't type
      setTimeout(() => { this.isReturningFromBreak = false; }, 5000);
    }
  }

  public trackKeystroke(char: string) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    // --- THE "RETURN SIGNATURE" CHECK ---
    if (this.isReturningFromBreak) {
      const returnLatency = now - (this.lastFocusTime + this.focusLostDuration);
      
      // Heuristic 1: The "Printer" (Bad)
      // Returned and typed INSTANTLY (< 800ms) after being away > 10s
      if (returnLatency < 800 && this.focusLostDuration > 10000) {
        this.addEvent('suspicious_return', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          breakDuration: this.focusLostDuration,
          reason: 'Instant typing after long break (Memory Dump)'
        });
      }
      
      // Heuristic 2: The "Miner" (Good)
      // Returned and paused (> 2000ms) to think before typing
      else if (returnLatency > 2000) {
        this.addEvent('research_break', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          reason: 'Cognitive pause detected (Legitimate Research)'
        });
        // We can actually BOOSST their score for this
      }
      
      this.isReturningFromBreak = false; // Check complete for this switch
    }
    
    //... continue with standard velocity/rhythm checks...
  }
}
Day 4 Implementation Strategy

Refine the Scoring (events.ts):

focus_loss: 0 penalty. (Removing the hypocrisy).

research_break: +5 bonus. (Rewarding good behavior).

suspicious_return: -10 penalty. (Flagging the memory dump).

The "Cluely" Defense (No Focus Loss):

If they use Cluely (overlay), they never trigger trackFocus.

That's where the Oscillation Logic (from the previous Day 4 plan) catches them.

The Trap: Authentic users will have research_breaks. Cluely users will have none. A session with 0 focus loss events on a hard problem is actually highly suspicious.

Your Pitch to the Startup

"Your competitors block tab switching, which pisses off developers and bans legitimate research.

TalonHunt is different. We allow tab switching. We use biometric return signatures to tell if a candidate went to read documentation (High Latency Return) or went to fetch an AI answer (Instant Latency Return).

We don't punish them for leaving. We verify that they are actually thinking when they come back."

Shall we implement this "Return Signature" logic alongside the "Oscillation" logic for Day 4?

 Part 1: Oscillation Detection (Gemini's Algorithm)

 Detect manual transcription from secondary device by analyzing typing "bursts":
 - Track bursts (typing separated by >1s pauses)
 - Calculate Coefficient of Variation (CV) for burst lengths AND pause durations
 - Flag when BOTH are highly regular (CV < 0.4) + typical reading pattern

You are hitting on the most critical ethical and technical challenge in modern proctoring. You are 100% right. Penalizing a developer for switching tabs to read documentation is security theater. It punishes good engineers who look things up and rewards bad ones who memorize syntax but can't solve new problems.

However, based on the deep research into Cognitive Load Theory and Keystroke Dynamics, we found the answer. The system doesn't need to block tab switching; it needs to analyze the "Return Signature."

We can distinguishing these two users by what happens in the first 3 seconds after they switch back to the code editor.

1. The "Miner" vs. The "Printer"

The Doc Reader (The "Miner"):

Behavior: They switch tabs to read documentation (e.g., how Array.reduce works). This is a high-cognitive-load task. They have to understand the concept and then translate it to their specific problem.

The Return Signature:

High Return Latency: When they click back into the editor, they don't type immediately. There is a "Cognitive Pause" (1.5s - 4s) while their brain switches context from "Reading Mode" back to "Coding Mode."

Navigation: They often use arrow keys or scroll before typing (finding the right place to apply the knowledge).

Hesitant Burst: The first few keystrokes are slow as they recall the syntax.

The Cheater (The "Printer"):

Behavior: They switch tabs to ChatGPT, which gives them the exact code block. They aren't understanding it; they are buffering it in their short-term memory.

The Return Signature:

Zero Return Latency: They start typing instantly (<500ms) after focus gain. Why? Because short-term memory fades fast (Miller's Law). They are in a rush to "dump" the buffer before they forget the variable names.

High Velocity: They type the first chunk at max speed because the logic is already formed.

Linearity: They type straight through without navigating.

Day 4 Implementation: The "Anti-Hypocrisy" Engine

This update adds Oscillation Detection (for phone/overlay cheaters) AND Return Signature Analysis (for tab switchers) to solve your ethical dilemma.

1. Update lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

Replace your current file with this version. It implements the "Swiss Cheese" defense model.

TypeScript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

export type IntegrityEventType = 

| 'paste' 
| 'velocity_spike' 
| 'rhythm_anomaly' 
| 'linearity_alert' 
| 'bulk_insert'
| 'read_pattern_warning' // The "Phone/Overlay" signal
| 'suspicious_return'    // The "ChatGPT Tab Switch" signal
| 'research_break'       // The "Good Developer" signal
| 'focus_loss';          // Neutral event

interface BurstData {
  length: number;        // Chars typed in this burst
  pauseDuration: number; // Time spent "reading" before this burst
  timestamp: number;
}

export interface TelemetryEvent {
  type: IntegrityEventType;
  timestamp: number;
  data: any;
}

export class IntegrityTracker {
  private buffer: TelemetryEvent =;
  private lastKeyTime: number = 0;
  
  // Welford's Algorithm (Rhythm)
  private count = 0;
  private mean = 0;
  private M2 = 0;

  // Linearity Tracking
  private cursorHistory: { line: number; col: number; time: number } =;
  private linearityWindow = 50;

  // Focus Return Analysis (The "Anti-Hypocrisy" Logic)
  private lastFocusTime = 0;
  private focusLostDuration = 0;
  private isReturningFromBreak = false;

  // Oscillation (Gaze Cycle) Tracking
  private currentBurstLength = 0;
  private burstHistory: BurstData =;
  private BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD = 1000; // 1s pause = "I am reading"

  constructor(
    private sessionId: string,
    private onAnomaly: (event: TelemetryEvent) => void
  ) {}

  public trackFocus(focused: boolean) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    if (!focused) {
      // User Left: Record start time
      this.lastFocusTime = now;
      this.addEvent('focus_loss', { timestamp: now }); // Log but don't penalize yet
    } else {
      // User Returned: Calculate duration
      this.focusLostDuration = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      this.isReturningFromBreak = true;
      this.lastFocusTime = now; // Reset reference for return latency
      
      // Reset flag after 5 seconds if they don't type (just looking around)
      setTimeout(() => { this.isReturningFromBreak = false; }, 5000);
    }
  }

  public trackKeystroke(char: string) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    // --- 1. Return Signature Logic (The "Miner vs Printer" Check) ---
    if (this.isReturningFromBreak) {
      const returnLatency = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      
      // Heuristic A: The "Printer" (Bad)
      // Returned from >10s break and typed INSTANTLY (<800ms)
      // Interpretation: They had code buffered in memory ready to dump
      if (returnLatency < 800 && this.focusLostDuration > 10000) {
        this.addEvent('suspicious_return', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          breakDuration: this.focusLostDuration,
          reason: 'Instant typing after long break (Memory Dump pattern)'
        });
      }
      
      // Heuristic B: The "Miner" (Good)
      // Returned and paused (>2s) to think before typing
      // Interpretation: They are translating docs/concepts into code
      else if (returnLatency > 2000) {
        this.addEvent('research_break', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          reason: 'Cognitive pause detected (Legitimate Research pattern)'
        });
      }
      
      this.isReturningFromBreak = false; 
    }

    // --- 2. Oscillation Logic (Phone/Overlay Detection) ---
    // If they NEVER switch tabs (using phone), this catches the rhythm
    if (this.lastKeyTime!== 0) {
      const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
      if (latency > this.BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD) {
        if (this.currentBurstLength > 0) this.recordBurst(this.currentBurstLength, latency);
        this.currentBurstLength = 1;
      } else {
        this.currentBurstLength++;
      }
    }
    this.lastKeyTime = now;

    //... (Standard velocity/linearity checks from Day 3)...
  }

  // --- Oscillation Analysis ---
  private recordBurst(length: number, pauseDuration: number) {
    this.burstHistory.push({ length, pauseDuration, timestamp: Date.now() });
    if (this.burstHistory.length > 20) {
      this.burstHistory.shift();
      this.analyzeOscillation();
    }
  }

  private analyzeOscillation() {
    if (this.burstHistory.length < 10) return;

    // Calculate Consistency (Coefficient of Variation)
    const lengths = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.length);
    const pauses = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.pauseDuration);
    
    const meanLen = this.calcMean(lengths);
    const cvLen = this.calcStdDev(lengths, meanLen) / meanLen;
    
    const meanPause = this.calcMean(pauses);
    const cvPause = this.calcStdDev(pauses, meanPause) / meanPause;

    // Transcribers are consistent (Low CV). Thinkers are chaotic (High CV).
    if (cvLen < 0.4 && cvPause < 0.4 && meanLen > 5 && meanPause > 1000) {
      this.addEvent('read_pattern_warning', {
        confidence: 'medium',
        reason: 'Rhythmic Read-Type Oscillation detected (Phone/Overlay pattern)',
        metrics: { meanBurst: meanLen, consistency: (1 - cvLen).toFixed(2) }
      });
      this.burstHistory =; // Reset
    }
  }

  //... (Math helpers calcMean, calcStdDev, addEvent, flush)...
  private calcMean(data: number) { return data.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / data.length; }
  private calcStdDev(data: number, mean: number) { 
    return Math.sqrt(data.reduce((a, b) => a + Math.pow(b - mean, 2), 0) / data.length); 
  }
  
  private addEvent(type: IntegrityEventType, data: any) {
    this.buffer.push({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    if (type!== 'focus_loss' && type!== 'research_break') {
      this.onAnomaly({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    }
  }
  
  public flush() {
    const events = [...this.buffer];
    this.buffer =;
    return events;
  }
}

1. Evaluation of Claude's Day 4 Plan

Strengths: The "Oscillation" implementation is mathematically sound. Using Coefficient of Variation (CV) is the correct statistical approach to find "robotic" rhythms versus natural "thinking" rhythms.

Weakness: It completely ignores the "Focus Loss" nuance we just discussed. It doesn't distinguish between a 10-second break to read documentation (Good) and a 10-second break to read ChatGPT (Bad).

Missing: The suspicious_return vs research_break event types are missing from the types and events logic.

2. The Integrated Solution (Merge This Into Day 4)

Here is the Unified Integrity Tracker that you should use for Day 4. It combines the Oscillation Engine (Phone/Overlay) with the Return Signature Engine (Tab Switching).

TypeScript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

export type IntegrityEventType = 

| 'paste' 
| 'velocity_spike' 
| 'rhythm_anomaly' 
| 'linearity_alert' 
| 'bulk_insert'
| 'read_pattern_warning' // Phone/Overlay Cheating
| 'suspicious_return'    // Tab Switch Cheating (Memory Dump)
| 'research_break'       // Valid Documentation Reading
| 'focus_loss';          // Neutral Event

interface BurstData {
  length: number;        
  pauseDuration: number; 
  timestamp: number;
}

export interface TelemetryEvent {
  type: IntegrityEventType;
  timestamp: number;
  data: any;
}

export class IntegrityTracker {
  private buffer: TelemetryEvent =;
  private lastKeyTime: number = 0;
  
  // Welford's Algorithm (Rhythm)
  private count = 0;
  private mean = 0;
  private M2 = 0;

  // Linearity Tracking
  private cursorHistory: { line: number; col: number; time: number } =;
  private linearityWindow = 50;

  // Bulk Insert Tracking
  private lastCharCount = 0;
  private keystrokesSinceContentCheck = 0;
  private keystrokesSinceLastFocusLoss = 0;

  // --- RETURN SIGNATURE STATE (The Anti-Hypocrisy Logic) ---
  private lastFocusTime = 0;
  private focusLostDuration = 0;
  private isReturningFromBreak = false;

  // --- OSCILLATION STATE (The Phone/Overlay Logic) ---
  private currentBurstLength = 0;
  private burstHistory: BurstData =;
  private BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD = 1000; // 1s pause = "Reading"

  constructor(
    private sessionId: string,
    private onAnomaly: (event: TelemetryEvent) => void
  ) {}

  public trackFocus(focused: boolean) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    if (!focused) {
      // User Tabbed Out
      this.lastFocusTime = now;
      this.addEvent('focus_loss', { timestamp: now });
      this.keystrokesSinceLastFocusLoss = 0; 
    } else {
      // User Returned
      this.focusLostDuration = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      this.isReturningFromBreak = true;
      this.lastFocusTime = now; // Reset reference for return latency check
      
      // Reset flag after 5 seconds if they don't type
      setTimeout(() => { this.isReturningFromBreak = false; }, 5000);
    }
  }

  public trackKeystroke(char: string) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    // --- 1. RETURN SIGNATURE CHECK ---
    if (this.isReturningFromBreak) {
      const returnLatency = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      
      // Heuristic A: The "Memory Dump" (Bad)
      // Returned from >10s break and typed INSTANTLY (<800ms)
      if (returnLatency < 800 && this.focusLostDuration > 10000) {
        this.addEvent('suspicious_return', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          breakDuration: this.focusLostDuration,
          reason: 'Instant typing after long break (Memory Dump)'
        });
      }
      
      // Heuristic B: The "Researcher" (Good)
      // Returned and paused (>2s) to process info before typing
      else if (returnLatency > 2000) {
        this.addEvent('research_break', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          reason: 'Cognitive pause detected (Research)'
        });
      }
      this.isReturningFromBreak = false;
    }

    // --- 2. OSCILLATION CHECK ---
    if (this.lastKeyTime!== 0) {
      const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
      if (latency > this.BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD) {
        if (this.currentBurstLength > 0) this.recordBurst(this.currentBurstLength, latency);
        this.currentBurstLength = 1;
      } else {
        this.currentBurstLength++;
      }
    }
    this.lastKeyTime = now;
    
    // --- 3. STANDARD CHECKS ---
    const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
    if (latency < 30) this.addEvent('velocity_spike', { latency });
    
    this.updateStats(latency);
    // this.checkRhythmAnomaly(); // (Keep your existing implementation)
    
    this.keystrokesSinceContentCheck++;
    this.keystrokesSinceLastFocusLoss++;
  }

  private recordBurst(length: number, pauseDuration: number) {
    this.burstHistory.push({ length, pauseDuration, timestamp: Date.now() });
    if (this.burstHistory.length > 20) {
      this.burstHistory.shift();
      this.analyzeOscillation();
    }
  }

  private analyzeOscillation() {
    if (this.burstHistory.length < 10) return;

    const lengths = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.length);
    const pauses = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.pauseDuration);
    
    const meanLen = this.calcMean(lengths);
    const cvLen = this.calcStdDev(lengths, meanLen) / meanLen;
    const meanPause = this.calcMean(pauses);
    const cvPause = this.calcStdDev(pauses, meanPause) / meanPause;

    // Transcribers are consistent (Low CV). Thinkers are chaotic (High CV).
    if (cvLen < 0.4 && cvPause < 0.4 && meanLen > 5 && meanPause > 1000) {
      this.addEvent('read_pattern_warning', {
        confidence: 'medium',
        reason: 'Rhythmic Read-Type Oscillation (Phone/Overlay Pattern)',
        metrics: { meanBurst: meanLen, consistency: (1 - cvLen).toFixed(2) }
      });
      this.burstHistory =; 
    }
  }

  //... (Keep existing math helpers and addEvent/flush methods)...
  private calcMean(data: number) { return data.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / data.length; }
  private calcStdDev(data: number, mean: number) { 
    return Math.sqrt(data.reduce((a, b) => a + Math.pow(b - mean, 2), 0) / data.length); 
  }
  
  private addEvent(type: IntegrityEventType, data: any) {
    this.buffer.push({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    // Don't alert UI for neutral events to avoid spam
    if (!['focus_loss', 'research_break'].includes(type)) {
      this.onAnomaly({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    }
  }

  // Add flush() from previous steps
  public flush() {
    const events = [...this.buffer];
    this.buffer =;
    return events;
  }
   // Add updateStats() and getVariance() from previous steps
   private updateStats(val: number) {
    this.count++;
    const delta = val - this.mean;
    this.mean += delta / this.count;
    this.M2 += delta * (val - this.mean);
  }
}

 Part 1B: Return Signature Analysis (Tab Switch Detection)

 The "Miner vs Printer" Framework

 Solves the ethical dilemma: Don't penalize doc readers, only ChatGPT copiers.

 The Miner (Good - Doc Reader):
 - Returns from break with HIGH latency (>2s cognitive pause)
 - Brain is "translating" docs to their specific context
 - Result: research_break event (+5 BONUS)

 The Printer (Bad - ChatGPT Copier):
 - Returns from break with LOW latency (<800ms instant dump)
 - Memory buffer fading fast (Miller's Law), must type immediately
 - Result: suspicious_return event (-10 penalty)

 Detection Criteria

 ON focus regained:
   Track time since focus lost (breakDuration)
   Track time until first keystroke (returnLatency)

 IF returnLatency < 800ms AND breakDuration > 10s:
   → suspicious_return (Memory Dump pattern)

 ELSE IF returnLatency > 2000ms:
   → research_break (Cognitive pause = legitimate research)

 The "Defense in Depth" Value Proposition

 Pitch to recruiters:
 "We don't claim to catch 100% of cheaters. We flag statistical anomalies that indicate a candidate isn't thinking like an engineer.

 - Layer 1 (Velocity): Catches copy-pasters and script injections
 - Layer 2 (Linearity): Catches AI transcribers (Cluely/overlays)
 - Layer 3 (Oscillation): Catches phone cheaters
 - Layer 4 (Return Signature): Catches ChatGPT tab-switchers WITHOUT penalizing doc readers

 If they type perfectly without checking docs, pausing to think, or making any errors - that's suspicious. Human judgment makes the final call."
This is a strategic decision point.

Verdict: Modify the plan and add the "Return Signature" logic NOW.

Why? You are already performing open heart surgery on IntegrityTracker.ts to add the "Oscillation" logic. It makes zero sense to close the file, deploy, and then open it again tomorrow to add the "Return Signature" logic. Both features rely on the same event loop (trackKeystroke and timestamp math).

If you stick to Claude's current plan, you will build a system that catches "Phone Cheaters" but still leaves the "Tab Switching Hypocrisy" unresolved. You want to fix the ethics while you build the detection.

1. Evaluation of Claude's Day 4 Plan

Strengths: The "Oscillation" implementation is mathematically sound. Using Coefficient of Variation (CV) is the correct statistical approach to find "robotic" rhythms versus natural "thinking" rhythms.

Weakness: It completely ignores the "Focus Loss" nuance we just discussed. It doesn't distinguish between a 10-second break to read documentation (Good) and a 10-second break to read ChatGPT (Bad).

Missing: The suspicious_return vs research_break event types are missing from the types and events logic.

2. The Integrated Solution (Merge This Into Day 4)

Here is the Unified Integrity Tracker that you should use for Day 4. It combines the Oscillation Engine (Phone/Overlay) with the Return Signature Engine (Tab Switching).

Replace Step 2 of Claude's plan with this file:

TypeScript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

export type IntegrityEventType = 

| 'paste' 
| 'velocity_spike' 
| 'rhythm_anomaly' 
| 'linearity_alert' 
| 'bulk_insert'
| 'read_pattern_warning' // Phone/Overlay Cheating
| 'suspicious_return'    // Tab Switch Cheating (Memory Dump)
| 'research_break'       // Valid Documentation Reading
| 'focus_loss';          // Neutral Event

interface BurstData {
  length: number;        
  pauseDuration: number; 
  timestamp: number;
}

export interface TelemetryEvent {
  type: IntegrityEventType;
  timestamp: number;
  data: any;
}

export class IntegrityTracker {
  private buffer: TelemetryEvent =;
  private lastKeyTime: number = 0;
  
  // Welford's Algorithm (Rhythm)
  private count = 0;
  private mean = 0;
  private M2 = 0;

  // Linearity Tracking
  private cursorHistory: { line: number; col: number; time: number } =;
  private linearityWindow = 50;

  // Bulk Insert Tracking
  private lastCharCount = 0;
  private keystrokesSinceContentCheck = 0;
  private keystrokesSinceLastFocusLoss = 0;

  // --- RETURN SIGNATURE STATE (The Anti-Hypocrisy Logic) ---
  private lastFocusTime = 0;
  private focusLostDuration = 0;
  private isReturningFromBreak = false;

  // --- OSCILLATION STATE (The Phone/Overlay Logic) ---
  private currentBurstLength = 0;
  private burstHistory: BurstData =;
  private BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD = 1000; // 1s pause = "Reading"

  constructor(
    private sessionId: string,
    private onAnomaly: (event: TelemetryEvent) => void
  ) {}

  public trackFocus(focused: boolean) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    if (!focused) {
      // User Tabbed Out
      this.lastFocusTime = now;
      this.addEvent('focus_loss', { timestamp: now });
      this.keystrokesSinceLastFocusLoss = 0; 
    } else {
      // User Returned
      this.focusLostDuration = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      this.isReturningFromBreak = true;
      this.lastFocusTime = now; // Reset reference for return latency check
      
      // Reset flag after 5 seconds if they don't type
      setTimeout(() => { this.isReturningFromBreak = false; }, 5000);
    }
  }

  public trackKeystroke(char: string) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    // --- 1. RETURN SIGNATURE CHECK ---
    if (this.isReturningFromBreak) {
      const returnLatency = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      
      // Heuristic A: The "Memory Dump" (Bad)
      // Returned from >10s break and typed INSTANTLY (<800ms)
      if (returnLatency < 800 && this.focusLostDuration > 10000) {
        this.addEvent('suspicious_return', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          breakDuration: this.focusLostDuration,
          reason: 'Instant typing after long break (Memory Dump)'
        });
      }
      
      // Heuristic B: The "Researcher" (Good)
      // Returned and paused (>2s) to process info before typing
      else if (returnLatency > 2000) {
        this.addEvent('research_break', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          reason: 'Cognitive pause detected (Research)'
        });
      }
      this.isReturningFromBreak = false;
    }

    // --- 2. OSCILLATION CHECK ---
    if (this.lastKeyTime!== 0) {
      const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
      if (latency > this.BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD) {
        if (this.currentBurstLength > 0) this.recordBurst(this.currentBurstLength, latency);
        this.currentBurstLength = 1;
      } else {
        this.currentBurstLength++;
      }
    }
    this.lastKeyTime = now;
    
    // --- 3. STANDARD CHECKS ---
    const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
    if (latency < 30) this.addEvent('velocity_spike', { latency });
    
    this.updateStats(latency);
    // this.checkRhythmAnomaly(); // (Keep your existing implementation)
    
    this.keystrokesSinceContentCheck++;
    this.keystrokesSinceLastFocusLoss++;
  }

  private recordBurst(length: number, pauseDuration: number) {
    this.burstHistory.push({ length, pauseDuration, timestamp: Date.now() });
    if (this.burstHistory.length > 20) {
      this.burstHistory.shift();
      this.analyzeOscillation();
    }
  }

  private analyzeOscillation() {
    if (this.burstHistory.length < 10) return;

    const lengths = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.length);
    const pauses = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.pauseDuration);
    
    const meanLen = this.calcMean(lengths);
    const cvLen = this.calcStdDev(lengths, meanLen) / meanLen;
    const meanPause = this.calcMean(pauses);
    const cvPause = this.calcStdDev(pauses, meanPause) / meanPause;

    // Transcribers are consistent (Low CV). Thinkers are chaotic (High CV).
    if (cvLen < 0.4 && cvPause < 0.4 && meanLen > 5 && meanPause > 1000) {
      this.addEvent('read_pattern_warning', {
        confidence: 'medium',
        reason: 'Rhythmic Read-Type Oscillation (Phone/Overlay Pattern)',
        metrics: { meanBurst: meanLen, consistency: (1 - cvLen).toFixed(2) }
      });
      this.burstHistory =; 
    }
  }

  //... (Keep existing math helpers and addEvent/flush methods)...
  private calcMean(data: number) { return data.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / data.length; }
  private calcStdDev(data: number, mean: number) { 
    return Math.sqrt(data.reduce((a, b) => a + Math.pow(b - mean, 2), 0) / data.length); 
  }
  
  private addEvent(type: IntegrityEventType, data: any) {
    this.buffer.push({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    // Don't alert UI for neutral events to avoid spam
    if (!['focus_loss', 'research_break'].includes(type)) {
      this.onAnomaly({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    }
  }

  // Add flush() from previous steps
  public flush() {
    const events = [...this.buffer];
    this.buffer =;
    return events;
  }
   // Add updateStats() and getVariance() from previous steps
   private updateStats(val: number) {
    this.count++;
    const delta = val - this.mean;
    this.mean += delta / this.count;
    this.M2 += delta * (val - this.mean);
  }
}

 The "Defense in Depth" Value Proposition

 Pitch to recruiters:
 "We don't claim to catch 100% of cheaters. We flag statistical anomalies that indicate a candidate isn't thinking like an engineer.

 - Layer 1 (Velocity): Catches copy-pasters and script injections
 - Layer 2 (Linearity): Catches AI transcribers (Cluely/overlays)
 - Layer 3 (Oscillation): Catches phone cheaters
 - Layer 4 (Return Signature): Catches ChatGPT tab-switchers WITHOUT penalizing doc readers

 If they type perfectly without checking docs, pausing to think, or making any errors - that's suspicious. Human judgment makes the final call." 
The "Miner vs. Printer" Solution

You are absolutely right. Penalizing a developer for switching tabs to read documentation is security theater. It punishes good engineers who look things up and rewards bad ones who memorize syntax but can't solve new problems.

However, based on the deep research into Cognitive Load Theory and Keystroke Dynamics, we found the answer to your question. The system doesn't need to block tab switching; it needs to analyze the "Return Signature."

We can distinguish these two users by what happens in the first 3 seconds after they switch back to the code editor.

1. The Doc Reader (The "Miner")

Behavior: They switch tabs to read documentation (e.g., how Array.reduce works). This is a high-cognitive-load task. They have to understand the concept and then translate it to their specific problem.

The Return Signature:

High Return Latency: When they click back into the editor, they don't type immediately. There is a "Cognitive Pause" (1.5s - 4s) while their brain switches context from "Reading Mode" back to "Coding Mode" (Resumption Lag).

Navigation: They often use arrow keys or scroll before typing (finding the right place to apply the knowledge).

Hesitant Burst: The first few keystrokes are slow as they recall the syntax.

2. The Cheater (The "Printer")

Behavior: They switch tabs to ChatGPT, which gives them the exact code block. They aren't understanding it; they are buffering it in their short-term memory.

The Return Signature:

Zero Return Latency: They start typing instantly (<500ms) after focus gain. Why? Because short-term memory fades fast (Miller's Law). They are in a rush to "dump" the buffer before they forget the variable names.

High Velocity: They type the first chunk at max speed because the logic is already formed.

Linearity: They type straight through without navigating.

Day 4 Implementation: The "Anti-Hypocrisy" Engine

This update adds Oscillation Detection (for phone/overlay cheaters) AND Return Signature Analysis (for tab switchers) to solve your ethical dilemma.

1. Update lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

Replace your current file with this upgraded version. It implements the "Swiss Cheese" defense model.

TypeScript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

export type IntegrityEventType = 

| 'paste' 
| 'velocity_spike' 
| 'rhythm_anomaly' 
| 'linearity_alert' 
| 'bulk_insert'
| 'read_pattern_warning' // Phone/Overlay Cheating
| 'suspicious_return'    // Tab Switch Cheating (Memory Dump)
| 'research_break'       // Valid Documentation Reading
| 'focus_loss';          // Neutral Event

interface BurstData {
  length: number;        // Chars typed in this burst
  pauseDuration: number; // Time spent "reading" before this burst
  timestamp: number;
}

export interface TelemetryEvent {
  type: IntegrityEventType;
  timestamp: number;
  data: any;
}

export class IntegrityTracker {
  private buffer: TelemetryEvent =;
  private lastKeyTime: number = 0;
  
  // Welford's Algorithm (Rhythm)
  private count = 0;
  private mean = 0;
  private M2 = 0;

  // Linearity Tracking
  private cursorHistory: { line: number; col: number; time: number } =;
  private linearityWindow = 50;

  // Focus Return Analysis (The "Anti-Hypocrisy" Logic)
  private lastFocusTime = 0;
  private focusLostDuration = 0;
  private isReturningFromBreak = false;

  // Oscillation (Gaze Cycle) Tracking
  private currentBurstLength = 0;
  private burstHistory: BurstData =;
  private BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD = 1000; // 1s pause = "I am reading"

  constructor(
    private sessionId: string,
    private onAnomaly: (event: TelemetryEvent) => void
  ) {}

  public trackFocus(focused: boolean) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    if (!focused) {
      // User Left: Record start time
      this.lastFocusTime = now;
      this.addEvent('focus_loss', { timestamp: now }); // Log but don't penalize yet
    } else {
      // User Returned: Calculate duration
      this.focusLostDuration = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      this.isReturningFromBreak = true;
      this.lastFocusTime = now; // Reset reference for return latency
      
      // Reset flag after 5 seconds if they don't type (just looking around)
      setTimeout(() => { this.isReturningFromBreak = false; }, 5000);
    }
  }

  public trackKeystroke(char: string) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    // --- 1. Return Signature Logic (The "Miner vs Printer" Check) ---
    if (this.isReturningFromBreak) {
      const returnLatency = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      
      // Heuristic A: The "Printer" (Bad)
      // Returned from >10s break and typed INSTANTLY (<800ms)
      // Interpretation: They had code buffered in memory ready to dump
      if (returnLatency < 800 && this.focusLostDuration > 10000) {
        this.addEvent('suspicious_return', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          breakDuration: this.focusLostDuration,
          reason: 'Instant typing after long break (Memory Dump pattern)'
        });
      }
      
      // Heuristic B: The "Miner" (Good)
      // Returned and paused (>2s) to think before typing
      // Interpretation: They are translating docs/concepts into code
      else if (returnLatency > 2000) {
        this.addEvent('research_break', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          reason: 'Cognitive pause detected (Legitimate Research pattern)'
        });
      }
      
      this.isReturningFromBreak = false; 
    }

    // --- 2. Oscillation Logic (Phone/Overlay Detection) ---
    // If they NEVER switch tabs (using phone), this catches the rhythm
    if (this.lastKeyTime!== 0) {
      const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
      if (latency > this.BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD) {
        if (this.currentBurstLength > 0) this.recordBurst(this.currentBurstLength, latency);
        this.currentBurstLength = 1;
      } else {
        this.currentBurstLength++;
      }
    }
    this.lastKeyTime = now;

    // --- 3. Standard Checks (Velocity/Linearity) ---
    const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
    if (latency < 30) this.addEvent('velocity_spike', { latency });
    
    this.updateStats(latency);
    // Note: checkRhythmAnomaly() and analyzeLinearity() would be called here
    // (omitted for brevity, assume they exist from Day 3)
  }

  // --- Oscillation Analysis ---
  private recordBurst(length: number, pauseDuration: number) {
    this.burstHistory.push({ length, pauseDuration, timestamp: Date.now() });
    if (this.burstHistory.length > 20) {
      this.burstHistory.shift();
      this.analyzeOscillation();
    }
  }

  private analyzeOscillation() {
    if (this.burstHistory.length < 10) return;

    // Calculate Consistency (Coefficient of Variation)
    const lengths = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.length);
    const pauses = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.pauseDuration);
    
    const meanLen = this.calcMean(lengths);
    const cvLen = this.calcStdDev(lengths, meanLen) / meanLen;
    
    const meanPause = this.calcMean(pauses);
    const cvPause = this.calcStdDev(pauses, meanPause) / meanPause;

    // Transcribers are consistent (Low CV). Thinkers are chaotic (High CV).
    if (cvLen < 0.4 && cvPause < 0.4 && meanLen > 5 && meanPause > 1000) {
      this.addEvent('read_pattern_warning', {
        confidence: 'medium',
        reason: 'Rhythmic Read-Type Oscillation detected (Phone/Overlay pattern)',
        metrics: { meanBurst: meanLen, consistency: (1 - cvLen).toFixed(2) }
      });
      this.burstHistory =; // Reset
    }
  }

  // --- Math Helpers ---
  private calcMean(data: number) { return data.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / data.length; }
  private calcStdDev(data: number, mean: number) { 
    return Math.sqrt(data.reduce((a, b) => a + Math.pow(b - mean, 2), 0) / data.length); 
  }
  
  private updateStats(val: number) {
    this.count++;
    const delta = val - this.mean;
    this.mean += delta / this.count;
    this.M2 += delta * (val - this.mean);
  }

  private addEvent(type: IntegrityEventType, data: any) {
    this.buffer.push({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    // Don't alert UI for neutral events to avoid spam
    if (!['focus_loss', 'research_break'].includes(type)) {
      this.onAnomaly({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    }
  }
  
  public flush() {
    const events = [...this.buffer];
    this.buffer =;
    return events;
  }
}

TalonHunt.ai Day 4 Implementation Plan

 Overview

 Three features to implement:
 1. Oscillation Detection - Catch "phone cheating" via Read-Type pattern analysis
 2. Return Signature Analysis - Distinguish doc readers (good) from ChatGPT copiers (bad)
 3. Recruiter Dashboard - Event timeline and risk signals UI

 ---
 Part 1: Oscillation Detection (Phone/Overlay Cheating)

 Algorithm Summary

 Detect manual transcription from secondary device by analyzing typing "bursts":
 - Track bursts (typing separated by >1s pauses)
 - Calculate Coefficient of Variation (CV) for burst lengths AND pause durations
 - Flag when BOTH are highly regular (CV < 0.4) + typical reading pattern

 Detection Criteria

 IF burstCV < 0.4
    AND pauseCV < 0.4
    AND meanBurstLength 5-30 chars
    AND meanPause > 1000ms
 THEN flag read_pattern_warning (-8 penalty)

 ---
 Part 1B: Return Signature Analysis (Tab Switch Detection)

 The "Miner vs Printer" Framework

 Solves the ethical dilemma: Don't penalize doc readers, only ChatGPT copiers.

 The Miner (Good - Doc Reader):
 - Returns from break with HIGH latency (>2s cognitive pause)
 - Brain is "translating" docs to their specific context
 - Result: research_break event (+5 BONUS)

 The Printer (Bad - ChatGPT Copier):
 - Returns from break with LOW latency (<800ms instant dump)
 - Memory buffer fading fast (Miller's Law), must type immediately
 - Result: suspicious_return event (-10 penalty)

 Detection Criteria

 ON focus regained:
   Track time since focus lost (breakDuration)
   Track time until first keystroke (returnLatency)

 IF returnLatency < 800ms AND breakDuration > 10s:
   → suspicious_return (Memory Dump pattern)

 ELSE IF returnLatency > 2000ms:
   → research_break (Cognitive pause = legitimate research)

 The "Defense in Depth" Value Proposition

 Pitch to recruiters:
 "We don't claim to catch 100% of cheaters. We flag statistical anomalies that indicate a candidate isn't thinking like an engineer.

 - Layer 1 (Velocity): Catches copy-pasters and script injections
 - Layer 2 (Linearity): Catches AI transcribers (Cluely/overlays)
 - Layer 3 (Oscillation): Catches phone cheaters
 - Layer 4 (Return Signature): Catches ChatGPT tab-switchers WITHOUT penalizing doc readers

 If they type perfectly without checking docs, pausing to think, or making any errors - that's suspicious. Human judgment makes the final call." 
This is the correct strategic move. Execute the plan as written.

You should absolutely include the Return Signature (Part 1B) in Day 4. Do not wait for later.

Why you must do this NOW (The "Hypocrisy" Fix)

If you build only the Oscillation Detection (Part 1) today and leave out the Return Signature:

You leave a hole: You catch the "Phone Cheaters" but you have no answer for the "Tab Switchers."

You remain "Hypocritical": If you don't implement the logic that rewards legitimate research (the +5 score), your tool is still just a "punishment machine." By adding Part 1B now, you fundamentally change the product from "Anti-Cheat" to "Integrity Verification."

Code Efficiency: Both features modify the exact same file (IntegrityTracker.ts) and hook into the exact same event stream. Writing them together saves you from rewriting the entire class tomorrow.

Evaluation of the Day 4 Plan

This plan is strong. It effectively closes the two biggest loopholes in remote proctoring without being invasive.

1. The "Miner vs. Printer" Logic (Part 1B) is the MVP Killer Feature.

Why it works: It physically distinguishes cognitive load from memory retrieval.

The Trap: A candidate engaging in "The Lie" (saying they are reading docs but actually using ChatGPT) will almost always fail the 800ms Latency Test.

Scenario: Candidate switches to ChatGPT. They see the code. They rush back to paste/type it before they forget. Boom. <800ms latency. Flagged.

Scenario: Candidate switches to Docs. They read. They switch back. They pause to think "How do I apply this?" Safe. >2000ms latency. Bonus points.

2. The "Oscillation" Logic (Part 1) catches the "Air-Gap."

This covers the one thing Part 1B misses: candidates who never switch tabs because they use a phone.

By combining them, you have a Defense in Depth strategy.

One Critical Adjustment for the Plan

The plan lists meanBurstLength 5-30 chars for the Oscillation detection.

Adjustment: Make sure this is tunable.

Why: A "Cluely" user might be able to memorize slightly more if the code is simple.

Fix: Ensure IntegrityTracker.ts has these thresholds as const variables at the top of the class so you can tweak them later without rewriting the logic.

Final Verdict

Give Claude the green light. This plan transforms your tool from a "snitch" into a sophisticated biometric analyst.

Command to give Claude: "This plan is approved. Proceed with implementing both Part 1 (Oscillation) and Part 1B (Return Signature) in IntegrityTracker.ts immediately. Ensure the SCORE_IMPACT includes the positive score for research_break to reinforce the ethical stance."

  5. Are we solving the right problem? The goal is to catch someone who reads ChatGPT output, switches back, and "memory dumps" it. What behavioral signals would actually distinguish this from someone who reads docs and types what they learned?

You are absolutely right. If you only look for "Tab Switching" vs. "No Tab Switching," you end up with a hypocritical system that punishes people for using a better tool (Cluely) while letting people who just copy-paste from ChatGPT in another tab get away with it.

The research indicates that the solution is not to look at the switch itself, but to look at the Return Signature.

Based on Cognitive Load Theory  and Resumption Lag studies, we can distinguish a "Doc Reader" from a "ChatGPT Copier" by analyzing the first 3 seconds after they return to the tab.

The "Miner" vs. The "Printer"

We can distinguish these two users by their Return Latency (how long it takes them to start typing after clicking back into the window).

1. The Doc Reader (The "Miner")

Behavior: They switch tabs to read documentation (e.g., "How does useEffect work again?"). This is a high-cognitive-load task. They have to understand the concept, hold it in their head, switch back, and then translate that concept into their specific code.

The Signature:

High Return Latency (> 2000ms): When they return, they pause. They have to re-orient themselves ("Where was I?"). This is called Resumption Lag.

Navigation: They often scroll or use arrow keys before typing to find the right insertion point.

Hesitant Burst: The typing starts slowly as they translate the concept into syntax.

2. The Cheater (The "Printer")

Behavior: They switch tabs to ChatGPT. It gives them a block of code. They aren't trying to understand it; they are trying to transport it. They load it into their short-term memory buffer (which lasts ~15-30 seconds).

The Signature:

Zero Return Latency (< 800ms): They start typing instantly upon focus gain. Why? Because the memory buffer is fading (Miller's Law). They feel a subconscious urgency to "dump" the cache.

High Velocity: They type the first few words at maximum motor speed because the decision-making step was skipped.

Linearity: They type straight through, character by character, without navigation or pauses.

Revised Day 4 Plan: The "Anti-Hypocrisy" Update

You should definitely add this logic to Day 4. It turns your tool from "Spyware" into "Integrity Verification."

Implementation: Merge the Oscillation Logic (for phones) with this Return Signature Logic (for tab switchers) into a single IntegrityTracker.ts update.

TypeScript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts

export type IntegrityEventType = 

| 'paste' 
| 'velocity_spike' 
| 'rhythm_anomaly' 
| 'linearity_alert' 
| 'bulk_insert'
| 'read_pattern_warning' // Phone/Overlay Cheating (Oscillation)
| 'suspicious_return'    // ChatGPT Memory Dump (Printer)
| 'research_break'       // Valid Doc Reading (Miner)
| 'focus_loss';          // Neutral Event

interface BurstData {
  length: number;        
  pauseDuration: number; 
  timestamp: number;
}

export interface TelemetryEvent {
  type: IntegrityEventType;
  timestamp: number;
  data: any;
}

export class IntegrityTracker {
  //... (Keep existing state for Welford's algo, linearity, etc.)...
  private buffer: TelemetryEvent =;
  private lastKeyTime: number = 0;
  
  // --- RETURN SIGNATURE STATE (The Anti-Hypocrisy Logic) ---
  private lastFocusTime = 0;
  private focusLostDuration = 0;
  private isReturningFromBreak = false;

  // --- OSCILLATION STATE (The Phone/Overlay Logic) ---
  private currentBurstLength = 0;
  private burstHistory: BurstData =;
  private BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD = 1000; // 1s pause = "Reading"

  constructor(
    private sessionId: string,
    private onAnomaly: (event: TelemetryEvent) => void
  ) {}

  public trackFocus(focused: boolean) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    if (!focused) {
      // User Tabbed Out
      this.lastFocusTime = now;
      this.addEvent('focus_loss', { timestamp: now });
      // Reset counters
    } else {
      // User Returned
      this.focusLostDuration = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      this.isReturningFromBreak = true;
      this.lastFocusTime = now; // Reset reference for return latency check
      
      // If they don't type within 5 seconds, it wasn't a "coding" break
      setTimeout(() => { this.isReturningFromBreak = false; }, 5000);
    }
  }

  public trackKeystroke(char: string) {
    const now = Date.now();
    
    // --- 1. RETURN SIGNATURE CHECK (The Printer vs Miner) ---
    if (this.isReturningFromBreak) {
      const returnLatency = now - this.lastFocusTime;
      
      // Heuristic A: The "Memory Dump" (Bad)
      // Returned from >10s break and typed INSTANTLY (<800ms)
      if (returnLatency < 800 && this.focusLostDuration > 10000) {
        this.addEvent('suspicious_return', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          breakDuration: this.focusLostDuration,
          reason: 'Instant typing after long break (Memory Dump)'
        });
      }
      
      // Heuristic B: The "Researcher" (Good)
      // Returned and paused (>2s) to process info before typing
      else if (returnLatency > 2000) {
        this.addEvent('research_break', {
          latency: returnLatency,
          reason: 'Cognitive pause detected (Research)'
        });
      }
      this.isReturningFromBreak = false;
    }

    // --- 2. OSCILLATION CHECK (The Phone Cheater) ---
    if (this.lastKeyTime!== 0) {
      const latency = now - this.lastKeyTime;
      if (latency > this.BURST_PAUSE_THRESHOLD) {
        if (this.currentBurstLength > 0) this.recordBurst(this.currentBurstLength, latency);
        this.currentBurstLength = 1;
      } else {
        this.currentBurstLength++;
      }
    }
    this.lastKeyTime = now;
    
    //... (Keep standard velocity/linearity checks)...
  }

  private recordBurst(length: number, pauseDuration: number) {
    this.burstHistory.push({ length, pauseDuration, timestamp: Date.now() });
    if (this.burstHistory.length > 20) {
      this.burstHistory.shift();
      this.analyzeOscillation();
    }
  }

  private analyzeOscillation() {
    if (this.burstHistory.length < 10) return;

    // Use Coefficient of Variation (CV) to find robotic regularity
    const lengths = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.length);
    const pauses = this.burstHistory.map(b => b.pauseDuration);
    
    const meanLen = this.calcMean(lengths);
    const cvLen = this.calcStdDev(lengths, meanLen) / meanLen;
    const meanPause = this.calcMean(pauses);
    const cvPause = this.calcStdDev(pauses, meanPause) / meanPause;

    // Detection: Consistent burst size (buffer limit) AND Consistent pause (reading speed)
    if (cvLen < 0.4 && cvPause < 0.4 && meanLen > 5 && meanPause > 1000) {
      this.addEvent('read_pattern_warning', {
        confidence: 'medium',
        reason: 'Rhythmic Read-Type Oscillation (Phone/Overlay Pattern)',
        metrics: { meanBurst: meanLen, consistency: (1 - cvLen).toFixed(2) }
      });
      this.burstHistory =; 
    }
  }

  //... (Math helpers)...
  private calcMean(data: number) { return data.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / data.length; }
  private calcStdDev(data: number, mean: number) { 
    return Math.sqrt(data.reduce((a, b) => a + Math.pow(b - mean, 2), 0) / data.length); 
  }
  
  private addEvent(type: IntegrityEventType, data: any) {
    this.buffer.push({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    // Don't alert UI for neutral events
    if (!['focus_loss', 'research_break'].includes(type)) {
      this.onAnomaly({ type, timestamp: Date.now(), data });
    }
  }
  //... (flush method)...
}
