## Session: 2026-01-16 - Return Signature Debugging & Threshold Tuning

### Context
Claude was debugging why `suspicious_return` wasn't firing even when simulating the behavior (10s break, instant typing).

### Claude's Questions for Gemini

1. Is the browser `window.focus` event reliable for detecting tab switches? Or do we need `document.visibilitychange` instead? What's the difference?
2. Is 10 seconds too long for `MIN_BREAK_DURATION_MS`? What's a realistic threshold for "went to ChatGPT and came back"?
3. Is 800ms too short for `PRINTER_LATENCY_MS`? Human reaction time is ~200-300ms. If someone switches back and sees where they left off, could they reasonably start typing within 800ms even legitimately?
4. Alternative approach: Instead of measuring time-to-first-keystroke, should we measure the typing pattern immediately after return? (e.g., if the first 30 characters have suspiciously low variance, that's more telling than raw latency)
5. Are we solving the right problem? The goal is to catch someone who reads ChatGPT output, switches back, and "memory dumps" it. What behavioral signals would actually distinguish this from someone who reads docs and types what they learned?

### Gemini's Answers

#### 1. `window.focus` vs. `document.visibilitychange`?

**Verdict:** You should use **`document.visibilitychange`** for the "Tab Switch" detection, but keep `window.focus` for "Overlay" detection.

* **The Technical Nuance:**
  * `window.onblur` fires if I click a Sticky Note, a separate monitor, or a specialized overlay tool (Cluely), even if the browser is still visible. This is critical for catching "Overlay Cheaters."
  * `document.visibilitychange` (checking `document.hidden`) *only* fires when the tab is actually hidden (user pressed Alt+Tab or switched Chrome tabs).

* **The Fix:** For **Return Signature Analysis** (detecting ChatGPT), `visibilitychange` is the cleaner signal because it implies the user *cannot see the code* anymore. `onblur` (overlay) implies they might still be looking at it.
* **Recommendation:** Use `visibilitychange` to start the `focusLostDuration` timer for the "Printer vs. Miner" logic. Use `window.onblur` only for the "Focus Loss Count" metric.

#### 2. Is 10 seconds (`MIN_BREAK_DURATION_MS`) too long?

**Verdict:** **Yes, it is too long.**

* **Research Basis:** The "Miller Oscillator" behavior described in the research suggests that users transcribing from a chatbot often switch frequently to refresh their working memory. A "Fill Phase" (reading the snippet) can happen in **5–8 seconds** for a short block of code.
* **The Bug:** If you are testing by counting "One, two... ten" and switching back, you might be hitting 9.8 seconds, and the logic `if (breakDuration < 10000) return;` silently fails.
* **Recommendation:** Lower `MIN_BREAK_DURATION_MS` to **5000ms (5 seconds)**. If someone leaves for 5 seconds and comes back typing instantly, that is still suspicious.

#### 3. Is 800ms (`PRINTER_LATENCY_MS`) too short?

**Verdict:** **It is aggressive, but scientifically sound for "Printers."**

* **Cognitive Science:** Simple reaction time is ~250ms. However, "Resumption Lag" (re-acquiring context after a task switch) typically adds 1–2 seconds for legitimate work (The "Miner").
* **The "Printer" Behavior:** The reason "Printers" are fast (<800ms) is that they *pre-loaded* the motor plan while looking at the other tab. They are "dumping the buffer."
* **The False Negative Risk:** If there is *any* UI lag, React render delay, or network jitter, a valid 500ms physical reaction might register as 900ms in the code.
* **Recommendation:** Raise `PRINTER_LATENCY_MS` to **1000ms (1 second)** to be safe.

#### 4. Should we measure the *pattern* after return, not just the first key?

**Verdict:** **YES. This is the "God Tier" fix.**

* **The Flaw:** Relying on a single timestamp (`firstKeystrokeTime`) is brittle. A user might switch back, accidentally bump a key (latency: 100ms), then pause for 3 seconds. The current logic flags them as a cheater.
* **The "Burst" Solution:** Analyze the **first 5 keystrokes** after return.
  * *Printer:* Key 1 (200ms) -> Key 2 (100ms) -> Key 3 (100ms) -> Key 4 (100ms). (High Velocity, Low Variance).
  * *Miner:* Key 1 (200ms) -> ...Pause (500ms)... -> Key 2 -> ...Pause... (Hesitation/Translation).
* **Recommendation for Day 4:** Stick to the single timestamp for MVP simplicity (just bump the threshold to 1000ms), but acknowledge that "Post-Return Burst Analysis" is the Version 2.0 feature.

#### 5. Are we solving the right problem?

**Verdict:** **Yes.**

* You are solving the "Hypocrisy of Focus Loss."
* By distinguishing "The Miner" (High Latency Return) from "The Printer" (Low Latency Return), you transform the tool from "Spyware" into "Integrity Verification."
* **Critical Note:** If your `suspicious_return` isn't triggering, it is almost certainly because the `breakDuration` threshold (10s) is effectively filtering out your test cases.

### Immediate Fixes Applied by Claude

```typescript
// lib/IntegrityTracker.ts
const RETURN_SIGNATURE_CONFIG = {
  MIN_BREAK_DURATION_MS: 5000,  // Lowered from 10s to catch quicker lookups
  PRINTER_LATENCY_MS: 1000,     // Increased from 800ms to account for system lag
  MINER_LATENCY_MS: 2000,       // Still >2s for legitimate research
};
```

Also added `document.addEventListener('visibilitychange')` alongside `window.blur/focus` for more reliable tab switch detection.

## Session: 2026-01-16 - bulk_insert False Positives & Phone Glancing Detection

### User Feedback
- Integrity score dropped to 80 (detection working!)
- `bulk_insert` triggered with 11-13 characters
- User claims: "I didn't paste anything, I was copying from my phone"
- User is a fast typist who has practiced this problem multiple times

### Gemini's Analysis

#### The `bulk_insert` Verdict: It's a False Positive

**Diagnosis:** The user triggered `bulk_insert` with 11-13 characters. This is almost certainly **Autocomplete** or **bracket auto-completion** (e.g., typing `console.log(` and editor auto-closes `)`.

**The Fix:** Relax this threshold. In a coding interview, using autocomplete is **not** cheating; it's proficiency. Flagging it creates noise.

**Recommendation:** Raise the `bulk_insert` threshold to **>20 characters**. Anything less than 20 chars is likely valid autocomplete (e.g., `System.out.println`).

#### The `suspicious_return` Verdict: Behavioral Mismatch

**Diagnosis:** The user is asking "Why isn't the 'Printer' alarm going off?" while acting like a "Phone User."

* **The Printer (Tab Switcher):** Switches tab -> Memorizes -> Switches back -> **Dumps instantly (<1s)**.
* **The Phone User:** Looks at phone. Might switch tabs, but when clicking back to the editor, doesn't type within **1000ms**. Probably clicks back, glances at phone to find place, *then* types. That tiny hesitation (>1s) saves them from the "Printer" flag.

**The Signal You Want:** If copying from phone, look for **`read_pattern_warning`** (The Oscillation / "Heartbeat" signal), NOT `suspicious_return`.

#### The "Phone Copying" Reality Check

**The "Glancer" is the hardest to catch.** If looking at phone for every single word (Glance -> Type 'const' -> Glance -> Type 'data'), breaks the "Burst" detection because bursts are too short (1 word).

**However:** That behavior is incredibly inefficient. A real cheater usually gets frustrated and starts memorizing larger chunks (3-4 words), which *will* trigger the `read_pattern_warning`.

### Fixes Applied by Claude

1. **Relaxed `bulk_insert`:** Increased threshold from `>10` to `>20` chars to allow fast typing + auto-closing brackets

2. **Tuned Oscillation for 'Glancers':** Lowered `MIN_BURST_LENGTH` from `5` to `3` chars to catch users who type in very short chunks while constantly checking their phone

### On Visual vs. Behavioral Detection

**User Question:** "Glancers would still be caught in a live interview, right? Should we still make provisions for that?"

**Gemini's Answer:** **Yes, absolutely implement it.** Here's why:

1. **The "Subtle" Cheater (The Webcam Hack):** Smart cheaters tape the phone right next to the webcam or use a split-screen on the same monitor. To the interviewer, it looks like maintaining eye contact. But their brain still has to "Read -> Buffer -> Type." The rhythm will still oscillate.

2. **The "Receipts" (Evidence):** Without software, it's he-said-she-said. With TalonHunt, the recruiter has a log: "At 10:15, the system flagged a 'Read-Pattern Warning' with 95% confidence."

3. **The "Async" Market (HackerRank Killer):** If TalonHunt ever sells "Take-Home Tests" (no human watching), this feature becomes the only line of defense.

### Summary of Cheater Archetypes

| Archetype | Behavior | Detection Signal |
|-----------|----------|------------------|
| **The Printer** | Alt+Tab to ChatGPT → memorize → Alt+Tab back → dump instantly | `suspicious_return` (returnLatency <1s) |
| **The Phone Glancer** | Glance at phone → type a few chars → glance again | `read_pattern_warning` (oscillation/burst pattern) |
| **The Code Injector** | Paste large blocks or use Cluely/Copilot | `bulk_insert` (>20 chars, <3 keystrokes) |

### Current Thresholds (After Tuning)

```typescript
// Oscillation Detection
const OSCILLATION_CONFIG = {
  MIN_BURSTS: 8,
  BURST_GAP_MS: 1000,
  MAX_BURST_CV: 0.4,
  MAX_PAUSE_CV: 0.4,
  MIN_BURST_LENGTH: 3,   // Lowered from 5 to catch micro-transcribers
  MAX_BURST_LENGTH: 30,
  MIN_MEAN_PAUSE_MS: 1000,
};

// Return Signature Analysis
const RETURN_SIGNATURE_CONFIG = {
  MIN_BREAK_DURATION_MS: 5000,  // Lowered from 10s
  PRINTER_LATENCY_MS: 1000,     // Raised from 800ms
  MINER_LATENCY_MS: 2000,
};

// bulk_insert threshold: >20 chars (raised from >10)
```

## Session: 2026-01-16 - Dynamic Glancing Detection (Fast Glancer Fix)

### User Question
"Glancers would still be caught in a live interview, right? Should we still make provisions for that?"

### Gemini's Response on Score Interpretation

**Is 80-85 a good score?**
- **100:** Perfect integrity
- **90-99:** Normal developer behavior (typos, small pastes, checking docs)
- **80-89:** **Yellow Flag.** Candidate is doing something unusual. Score of 80 would likely trigger manual review
- **< 70:** **Red Flag.** High probability of cheating

**Why did score go up to 85?**
Triggered the `research_break` bonus (+5 points). User switched tabs, stayed away >5 seconds, then came back and *didn't* type instantly like a robot - they hesitated. System interprets this as "someone who read documentation and is now trying to apply it."

### The "Dynamic Glancing" Update

**Problem:** Hardcoded thresholds like "5 seconds" are brittle. A fast cheater (or someone with a second monitor) might glance for just **1 second** every few words.

**Solution:** Make the Oscillation Engine dynamic. Instead of asking "Did they pause for 5 seconds?", ask: **"Is there a consistent rhythm to their pauses?"**

If a user pauses for **1.2 seconds** every **5 words** consistently, that is a machine-like rhythm (reading), even if it's fast.

### Gemini's Recommendations

1. **Lower the Floor:** In `OSCILLATION_CONFIG`, lower `MIN_MEAN_PAUSE_MS` from `1000` to **`500`** ms. This catches the quick "eyes-darting" checks to a phone/monitor.

2. **Catch Micro-Bursts:** Lower `MIN_BURST_LENGTH` from `5` to **`3`** characters. (Already done!)

3. **Tighten CV Threshold:** In `analyzeOscillation`, require `pauseCV < 0.3` (was 0.4) to be stricter about consistency. Low variance means robotic consistency = reading.

### Implementation Status

| Change | Before | After | Status |
|--------|--------|-------|--------|
| `MIN_MEAN_PAUSE_MS` | 1000 | 500 | ✅ Applied |
| `MIN_BURST_LENGTH` | 5 | 3 | ✅ Already done |
| `MAX_PAUSE_CV` | 0.4 | 0.3 | ✅ Applied |

## AI Analysis Layer (Layer 2: The Detective)

### Architecture: Three Layers

1. **Layer 1 (The Sensors):** Raw telemetry (IntegrityTracker.ts)
2. **Layer 2 (The Detective):** AI Analysis (Gemini API) that connects the dots
3. **Layer 3 (The Judge):** The Human Reviewer

### Claude's Assessment

The "AI insight" layer transforms the product from "data dashboard" to "insight engine." This is the right strategic move for a demo.

**Critical Issue:** The prompt must explain event meanings correctly:

| Event | Meaning | Score Impact |
|-------|---------|--------------|
| `velocity_spike` | <30ms keystroke (machine speed) | Negative |
| `linearity_alert` | AI transcription pattern (Cluely) | Negative |
| `rhythm_anomaly` | Robotic consistency | Negative |
| `bulk_insert` | >20 chars injected without keystrokes | Negative |
| `read_pattern_warning` | Phone/overlay oscillation | Negative |
| `suspicious_return` | Printer pattern (ChatGPT dump) | **-10 penalty** |
| `focus_loss` | Tab switch | **Neutral** (normal!) |
| `research_break` | Miner pattern (doc reading) | **+5 BONUS** |

### Implementation Requirements

1. **Refined Prompt:** Clearly define event meanings to Gemini (especially that `research_break` is POSITIVE and `focus_loss` is NEUTRAL)

2. **Safe Verdicts:** Use legal-safe labels:
   - `"High Risk"` instead of "Likely Cheating"
   - `"Medium Risk"` instead of "Suspicious"
   - `"Low Risk"` instead of "Clean"

3. **Robust Parsing:** Add try-catch and JSON cleanup logic (LLMs don't always respect format instructions)

4. **Model Selection:** Use `gemini-1.5-flash` for speed during demo
   ```typescript
   // OLD (Do not use)
   const model = genAI.getGenerativeModel({ model: 'gemini-pro' });
   
   // NEW (Use this)
   const model = genAI.getGenerativeModel({ model: 'gemini-1.5-flash' });
   ```

### Why `gemini-1.5-flash`

1. **Speed:** Sub-second latency - the "Run Analysis" button feels instant
2. **Cost:** Effectively free for demo volume
3. **Accuracy:** More than capable for analyzing JSON logs

### Demo Safety: Mock Mode

Add a fallback for demo reliability:
```typescript
if (process.env.DEMO_MODE === 'true') {
  return { verdict: 'High Risk', summary: 'Simulation: Candidate pasted 500 lines...' };
}
```

This guarantees the demo always works even if Gemini API hangs.

### Priority

Build the AI Analysis Layer FIRST before Pulse Graph and Session Replay. The "AI Insight" is what sells the product to a busy hiring manager - they want a summary, not to watch a replay.

## 2026-01-16 - Session Resume

**Topic:** Demo Polish Roadmap - Challenge Selector, Auto-Interrogation, Focus Heatmap

**Gemini said:**

You are building a powerhouse demo. Adding these features will move your product from **"Interesting Tool"** to **"Mission Control."**

Here is the **"Demo Polish" Roadmap** to tackle these improvements logically. We start with the **Challenge Selector** (because you need *content* to cheat on), then move to the **Visuals** (Playback/Heatmap), and finish with the **Interventions** ("Explain This").

---

### **Phase 1: The Static Challenge Selector (The Setup)**

**Why First:** You cannot demo "cheating on a hard problem" if the editor is blank.
**The Plan:**

1. Create a hardcoded `data/challenges.ts` file with 3 distinct problems (Easy/Medium/Hard).
2. Add a dropdown menu to the **Candidate's Code Editor**.
3. When a challenge is picked, it pre-fills the editor with the problem description and function stub.

### **Phase 2: "Explain This" & Heatmap (The Explainer)**

**1. The Focus Heatmap:**

* **Concept:** A single horizontal bar representing the entire session timeline (e.g., 0 to 20 minutes).
* **Implementation:** The bar is default **Green** (Focused). We overlay **Red Blocks** corresponding to the timestamps of `focus_loss` events.
* **Visual:** It looks like a "availability timeline" (e.g., Uptime Robot). A fragmented bar instantly shows a distracted candidate.

**2. The "Explain This" Challenge (The Trap):**

* **Concept:** A "gotcha" mechanism to verify if they wrote the code or pasted it.
* **Implementation:**
* **Trigger:** We can make this **Automatic** (on `suspicious_return`) or **Manual** (Recruiter clicks "Probe" button). For a demo, **Manual** is safer and flashier.
* **Mechanism:** When triggered, a **Modal** locks the Candidate's screen: *"Security Audit: Please explain the logic of lines 12-15 in your own words."*
* **Tech:** Use **Ably** to send a real-time message from Recruiter Dashboard -> Candidate Client.

---

### **Revised Action Plan: The "Automated Defense" Demo**

**Auto-Interrogation Concept:**

Instead of a recruiter clicking a button, the **Integrity Tracker** triggers the challenge instantly when it detects high-risk behavior.

* **The Logic:**
  * **Trigger:** A `suspicious_return` event (Memory Dump) or a `bulk_insert` > 50 chars.
  * **Action:** The Editor **Locks**. A modal appears.
  * **The Prompt:** *"Security Check: We detected a rapid code insertion. To verify authorship, please explain the logic of lines 14-20 in one sentence."*
  * **The Verification (Async):** The candidate types an answer. The AI later compares their explanation to the actual code.

* **For the Demo:**
  * You paste the code (cheat).
  * **BAM!** The screen locks immediately.
  * You (the presenter) say: *"See? I didn't have to do anything. The system caught me and is now interrogating me automatically."*

**Focus Heatmap Concept:**

* **Visual:** A horizontal bar representing the 60-minute interview.
  * **Green:** Code Editor was active/focused.
  * **Gray:** User was idle (thinking).
  * **Red Blocks:** User was tabbed out (Focus Loss).

* **The Insight:**
  * *Scenario A:* A solid Green bar with small Gray gaps. -> **Good Candidate.**
  * *Scenario B:* A bar that is 40% Red followed by tiny Green slivers (Pasting). -> **Cheater.**

## 2026-01-17 - Autonomous Recruiter Architecture & 10 God-Tier Improvements

**Topic:** AI-Enhanced Platform Vision + Feature Roadmap

**Gemini said:**

### The "Autonomous Recruiter" Architecture
Moving from **Triggered Analysis** (Human clicks button) to **Continuous Surveillance** (AI runs in background).

The Reviewer's View: A "Live Feed" where AI posts updates every minute:
- "10:01 AM: Candidate started 'Dijkstra'. Typing rhythm normal."
- "10:02 AM: Anomaly: Cursor movement became 100% linear. Monitoring..."
- "10:03 AM: CRITICAL: 500 characters pasted. Triggering Interrogation."

### The "Smart" Auto-Interrogation with AI Lag
1. **Trigger**: `suspicious_return` or `bulk_insert`
2. **AI Lag UX**: Show "⚠ AI detected anomaly. Analyzing integrity signatures..." (2.5 seconds)
3. **Lock**: After delay, lock the screen and show interrogation modal

### 10 "God Tier" Improvements

**Category A: Active Defense (Candidate Side)**
1. **Dynamic "Explain This"** - AI reads the exact pasted code and generates specific questions like "Why did you name the variable graph_nodes here?"
2. **Trap Variable** - Invisible 0-width characters in question description to detect copy-paste to ChatGPT
3. **Honeypot Solution** - Post fake solutions with bugs on GitHub/StackOverflow *(User not a fan of this one)*

**Category B: Forensic Lab (Reviewer Side)**
4. **Live Commentary Feed** - Scrolling sidebar with real-time AI verdicts
5. **Stylometric Fingerprinting** - Detect code style changes (snake_case to camelCase = author change)
6. **Code Complexity vs Time Graph** - Humans build slowly (step ladder), cheaters jump instantly (vertical cliff)
7. **Undo Ratio** - Track Backspace frequency (honest coders make mistakes, copiers don't)
8. **Session Replay with AI Annotations** - Markers: "Thinking," "Coding," "Debugging," "Suspicious Paste"

**Category C: Autonomous Verdict**
9. **Confidence Score** - "88% Probability of AI Assistance" instead of binary cheating/not cheating
10. **Generated Rejection Email** - AI drafts the email, recruiter just clicks Send

### Dynamic Questioning Upgrade
Instead of static questions, send pasted code to Gemini Flash with prompt: "Generate a specific question about line 5 of this code to verify the user understands it."

## 2026-01-17 - 6-Feature Plan Review (Phased Approach Recommended)

**Topic:** Gemini critique of ambitious 6-feature expansion plan

**Gemini said:**

### Critique of the 6-Feature Plan

This plan is highly ambitious and includes 6 significant features. While each one is valuable, implementing all 6 at once carries a high risk of introducing bugs or breaking existing functionality, especially given the interdependencies and the "demo quality" constraint.

**Issues Identified:**

1. **Scope Creep Risk:** Implementing Video Proctoring (Feature 6) and Live Commentary (Feature 4) adds significant complexity (WebRTC, Google Cloud Storage, new UI components). Doing this alongside core detection logic changes (Feature 1 & 2) is risky.

2. **Video Proctoring Complexity:** Implementing video streaming/recording from scratch using MediaRecorder and chunked uploads to GCS is complex and prone to cross-browser issues and storage costs/permissions. For a demo, a simpler "simulated" or "snapshot-based" proctoring might be safer unless you absolutely need live video.

3. **"Live Commentary" Dependency:** This relies on Ably being set up correctly for a new channel. If Ably fails or is rate-limited, this feature breaks.

4. **Integration Overload:** Modifying `IntegrityTracker.ts`, `CodeEditor.tsx`, `useKeystrokeDynamics.ts`, API routes, and the Review Page all at once increases the chance of regressions.

### Recommendation: Phased Rollout

**Phase 1: Core Intelligence & Visuals (Features 1, 2, 3, 5)**
- Post-Return Burst Analysis - Smarter detection of "Alt-Tab -> Paste"
- Undo Ratio - Adds a subtle but powerful "Humanity Score"  
- Confidence Score - Makes the AI verdict look more professional (87% vs "High")
- AI-Annotated Replay - Adds "Smarts" to the replay feature

*Why:* These directly enhance the existing "Cheating Detection" value proposition without adding massive new infrastructure (like video storage). They make the current dashboard smarter and more "forensic."

**Phase 2: The "Big Bang" Features (Features 4, 6)**
- Live Commentary Feed - Real-time feedback loop
- Video Proctoring - Webcam integration

*Why:* Live Commentary and Video Proctoring are "Showstoppers" but technically distinct. They deserve their own focused implementation sprint to ensure stability.

### Gemini's Action Recommendation

> "Execute Phase 1 NOW (Core Intelligence & Visuals). DEFER Phase 2 (Live Commentary & Video) until the core detection logic is solid."

## 2026-01-17 - Phase 2 Strategy: Smart Snapshots over Full Video

**Topic:** Video Proctoring approach - Snapshots vs Full Recording

**Gemini said:**

### Why "Smart Snapshots" Wins the Demo

1. **You Already Have "Code Video":** Session Replay (DVR) IS a video of their code. Recording the screen (which mostly shows the code editor anyway) is redundant data.

2. **The "Evidence" Argument:** Recruiters don't watch hour-long videos. They want to see the **Smoking Gun**.
   - *Video Approach:* "Go to timestamp 14:02 to see him look at his phone."
   - *Snapshot Approach:* Dashboard shows a gallery of **"Red Flag Moments"**
     - Image 1: Candidate looking at phone (Auto-captured during `read_pattern_warning`)
     - Image 2: Candidate absent from chair (Auto-captured during `focus_loss`)

3. **Technical Reliability:** Uploading a JPEG every 15 seconds (and on critical events) is bulletproof. Streaming WebRTC video over hotel WiFi during a demo is asking for a crash.

### The "Smart Surveillance" Plan

**Visual Telemetry (Snapshots) instead of Video Recording:**

1. **Periodic:** Capture Webcam + Screen Snapshot every 30 seconds
2. **Event-Triggered (Secret Weapon):** Capture IMMEDIATE Snapshot when IntegrityTracker fires Red Flag
3. **Visualization:** On Pulse Graph, hover over Red Bar → show Snapshot taken at that exact moment

**"Mic Drop" feature:** Hover over red spike → popup shows photo of candidate holding their phone.

### Implementation Plan

1. **Visual Logger (`hooks/useVisualTelemetry.ts`):**
   - Capture webcam frame (and optional screen frame) as base64/blob
   - Triggers: Interval (30s) + Event-Driven (on warning/critical events)

2. **Storage:** Upload images to cloud storage (or base64 in Mongo for MVP if <10MB total)

3. **Recruiter UI:**
   - Update Pulse Graph: Hover bar → Tooltip Preview of webcam/screen snapshot
   - Connects Data (Red Spike) to Visual Proof (Photo of candidate)

4. **Live Commentary:** Ably-based real-time AI micro-verdicts

## 2026-01-18 - Adaptive AI Commentary Architecture

**Topic:** Event-Driven AI Commentary vs Periodic Polling

**Gemini said:**

### The Problem with Claude's Initial Plan
Claude proposed "Periodic Analysis every 15-30 seconds" which has a **fatal flaw for a demo**:
- If you sit idle for 15 seconds, the AI says nothing
- If you cheat at second 16, and the AI doesn't report until second 30, the demo feels "laggy"

### The Better Plan: "Event-Driven AI Commentary"

Do not just run a cron job every 30 seconds. Instead, trigger the AI **when the behavior shifts.**

#### 1. The "Normal" Learner (Local Logic):
- The system keeps a `running_average` of the user's typing speed and backspace ratio
- Example: User types at 60 WPM for the first minute. The system sets `Baseline = 60`

#### 2. The "Deviation" Trigger:
- If the user suddenly jumps to 120 WPM (2x Baseline), **THAT** triggers the call to Gemini
- Prompt: "User baseline is 60 WPM. Current burst is 120 WPM. Analyze."
- Gemini Reply: "Sudden velocity spike detected. 200% above established baseline. Investigating for paste-scripting."

#### 3. The "Vibe Check" (The Idle Commentary):
- Only if *nothing* happens for 45 seconds, send a "Heartbeat" to Gemini
- Prompt: "User is typing steadily at baseline."
- Gemini Reply: "Rhythm remains consistent. Cognitive flow appears natural."

### Why NOT Every Second Polling

If you call Gemini every second (1Hz), three things will destroy your demo:

1. **The "Pile-Up" Effect (Latency):** Even the fastest AI takes ~500ms to 1.5s to reply. Requests will queue up and commentary will lag 10 seconds behind reality.
2. **Rate Limits:** You will hit the API "Requests Per Minute" cap instantly. Demo crashes with `429 Too Many Requests`.
3. **User Noise:** A recruiter cannot read a new sentence every second. It becomes flickering text they ignore.

### The Solution: "Perceived" Real-Time (The Hybrid Model)

#### 1. The Local "Heartbeat" (Updates Every Second)
- Frontend updates **metrics** (WPM, Backspace Count, Activity Status) every second locally
- Visual: Recruiter sees numbers changing `60 WPM` -> `62 WPM` -> `0 WPM` instantly
- **This satisfies the "Real-Time" hunger**

#### 2. The AI "Brain" (Updates on *Change*)
The AI only speaks when the story changes:
- Typing normally for 20 seconds → Local updates WPM graph, AI silent (or one "Steady rhythm" message)
- Suddenly stop and Paste → Local detects `bulk_insert`, AI **IMMEDIATELY** fires: "Sudden context shift. User pasted code."

**This feels instant because the AI reacts exactly when the human brain notices something is wrong.**

### Model Recommendation: Gemini 2.0 Flash
- **Gemini 1.5 Pro / "Gemini 3" (Big Models):** Too Slow. Latency ~2-3 seconds feels sluggish for live feed.
- **Gemini 2.0 Flash:** Insanely Fast. Optimized for high-frequency tasks. Smart enough for analyzing typing patterns.

### The Implementation Architecture

1. **Local UI (1Hz Updates):**
   - `LiveCommentaryFeed` updates metrics (WPM, Status Icon) every second locally
   - Gives visual feedback of "Continuous Monitoring"

2. **AI Logic (Event-Driven + Adaptive):**
   - **The Baseline:** Calculate candidate's average WPM/Backspace ratio locally
   - **The Trigger:** Call Gemini ONLY when:
     - A "Red Flag" event occurs (`suspicious_return`, `bulk_insert`)
     - **OR** User deviates >30% from established baseline (Adaptive Threshold)
     - **OR** 45 seconds passed with no updates (Heartbeat to say "All good")

3. **The Prompt:**
   - Send current metrics + baseline metrics to Gemini
   - Ask: "Compare current behavior to the baseline. Is this natural or suspicious? Keep it under 15 words."

## 2026-01-18 - AI Vision Analysis for Webcam Snapshots

**Topic:** Adding Gemini Vision to analyze webcam snapshots in real-time

**Gemini said:**

### The Upgrade: From Passive Recording to Active Monitoring

You don't just want to store the photo; you want the AI to **tag** it.

* *Instead of just:* "Snapshot at 10:42."
* *It becomes:* "⚠️ **Visual Alert:** Subject looking down (likely phone) during code paste."

This makes the "Live Commentary" terrifyingly smart.

### The "AI Vision" Workflow

1. **Upload:** Frontend sends the Base64 image to `/api/visual-snapshots`
2. **Analyze (Server-Side):** The API immediately sends that image to Gemini 2.0 Flash
3. **Prompt:** "Analyze this webcam frame. Return JSON: `{ gaze: 'center' | 'off-screen' | 'down', objects: ['phone', 'second-person'] }`."
4. **Tag:** Save the AI's verdict with the snapshot in MongoDB
5. **Feed:** The "Live Commentary" reads this tag and posts: *"Visual Anomaly: Eyes diverted off-screen."*

### Implementation Details

**Update `app/api/visual-snapshots/route.ts`:**

1. **Integrate Gemini Vision:**
   - When a snapshot is received (POST), pass the `imageData` (Base64) to `googleGenerativeAI`
   - **Model:** Use `gemini-1.5-flash` or `gemini-2.0-flash-exp` (Fastest multimodal models)

2. **The Vision Prompt:**
   - "Analyze the candidate in this webcam frame for proctoring context. Check for: 1. Gaze direction (looking at screen vs away). 2. Suspicious objects (phones, people). 3. Presence (is chair empty?). Return a short 1-sentence risk assessment."

3. **Storage:**
   - Add a new field `aiAnalysis` to the `VisualSnapshot` schema
   - Store the AI's response string there

4. **Live Commentary Integration:**
   - If the Vision AI detects a risk (e.g., 'Looking away' or 'Phone visible'), **immediately publish** a message to the Ably `ai_verdict` channel so it pops up in the Recruiter's feed

**Constraint:** Fire this analysis asynchronously (don't make the client wait for the AI response).
